# @mymandev/desktop

The desktop application: an Electron shell with a React renderer. It shows the product UI skeleton, the status of the local runtime in the main process, the local Git repositories mounted on this device, the sign-in of this installation, the account's devices, the account's workspaces and projects, the people of the selected workspace, and the chat and the agents and runs of the open project, which the renderer reads through a typed IPC contract; the tasks, decisions, and permission screens show placeholder content. The decisions behind it are recorded in [ADR 0016](../../docs/architecture/decisions/0016-desktop-toolchain.md).

## Start locally

From the repository root, after `npm ci`:

```sh
npm run dev -w @mymandev/desktop
```

The first start downloads the Electron binary for your platform (about 130 MB on macOS) and checks it against the SHA-256 checksums that the Electron npm package ships. Later starts reuse it. electron-vite then builds the main process and the preload script, starts the Vite development server on `http://localhost:5173`, and opens the application window. A change in the renderer reloads in place. A change in the main process or the preload script takes effect after a restart; electron-vite's `--watch` option (`npm run dev -w @mymandev/desktop -- --watch`) rebuilds and restarts them on change instead. Stop it with Ctrl+C.

Some shells and tools set the environment variable `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`. With it, Electron runs as plain Node.js, and the start fails with "The requested module 'electron' does not provide an export named". Unset it before `npm run dev`, for example with `unset ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` on Linux and macOS, or `Remove-Item Env:ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` in PowerShell.

## Scripts

| Script      | Runs                                                        |
| ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| `dev`       | `install-electron && electron-vite dev`                     |
| `build`     | `electron-vite build`                                       |
| `typecheck` | `tsc -p tsconfig.json && tsc -p src/renderer/tsconfig.json` |
| `test`      | `vitest run`                                                |

Run them from the repository root with `-w @mymandev/desktop`, or from `apps/desktop`. `build` writes the bundles to `out/`: `out/main/index.js` (an ES module), `out/preload/index.cjs`, and `out/renderer/`. The type check, the tests, and the build do not need the Electron binary. `npx --no-install electron-vite preview` builds the application and starts it from `out/` the way a build runs: the renderer loads from `app://renderer/`.

## Layout

| Path                      | Contents                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `src/main/`               | The main process: the data folder, the single-instance lock, the application window, the `app://` scheme, the navigation rules, the guards for web contents and the session, the local runtime, the mounted repositories, the IPC handlers, the [device key store](#device-key) (`src/main/device-key/`), the [sign-in](#sign-in-flow) (`src/main/sign-in/`), the [account](#account), the [device list](#device-list-service) (`src/main/devices/`), and the [connection](#connection) (`src/main/connection/`). |
| `src/preload/`            | The preload script. It exposes `window.desktop` to the renderer.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `src/renderer/`           | The renderer: `index.html` with the Content Security Policy, the React entry point, the shell, the screens, the screen state, and the stylesheet. It has its own `tsconfig.json`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `src/shared/`             | Code and types that several sides use: the product name and the names of the data folder and the local data folder, the Content Security Policy, the `window.desktop` interface, the IPC channel names, and the IPC contract.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `config/csp.ts`           | The Vite plugin that writes the Content Security Policy into `index.html`, with the development server's additions during `npm run dev`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `electron.vite.config.ts` | The electron-vite configuration for the three targets.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

## Screens

The renderer shows the product UI skeleton (M0-04): the main screens, so that later work has a defined place for its content. The screens show placeholder content, except that the chat screen shows the open project's [chat](#chat-screen), the account screen the [account panel](#account), the people screen the selected workspace's [people](#people-screen), and the join screen the [invitation code](#join-screen); the sidebar shows the account's [workspaces](#workspaces) and the selected workspace's projects, the agent side panel shows the open project's [agents and runs](#agent-activity-service), and the repository side panel shows the [mounted repositories](#repositories). Nothing runs an agent; the main process connects to the cloud API once a device is signed in ([Connection](#connection)).

The screens are a typed state, not routes ([ADR 0016](../../docs/architecture/decisions/0016-desktop-toolchain.md)): [`src/renderer/screens.ts`](src/renderer/screens.ts) lists them, and `shellReducer` switches between them. A workspace contains projects ([ADR 0005](../../docs/architecture/decisions/0005-workspaces-contain-projects.md)), so the shell shows either a workspace screen or a screen of the open project.

| Screen        | Belongs to    | Shows                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ------------- | ------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `home`        | the workspace | A hint to open a project, and while no device is signed in and ready (workspace state `signed_out`), a fixed sentence about the sign-in and "Go to Account", which moves the focus to the account screen's title, with "Sign in with GitHub" before it while the account panel offers a sign-in that can start now (#219).                                                                                                                                                                |
| `account`     | the workspace | The [account panel](#account), headed "This device": sign in with GitHub, also in another browser, the pairing code, the login with "This is not me", the sign-in's end and renewal, and the passphrase of a device key that needs one. Beside it, the [devices section](#devices-section), headed "Devices": the account's devices, the registration lock, the notices of new devices, and the device actions.                                                                           |
| `people`      | the workspace | The [people screen](#people-screen): the selected workspace's people with their roles and GitHub logins, the account's own person marked, and the former members apart, with the people actions that the account's role allows. For the owner and the admins also the open invitations with "Revoke", "Invite" on each unclaimed person, and the code of a new invitation, shown once. Join requests follow (#95): their main process part is in place ([Join requests](#join-requests)). |
| `join`        | the workspace | The [join screen](#join-screen): joining a workspace with an invitation code, previewed first and accepted after a question. The request to join by a workspace ID and the account's own join requests follow (#95).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `about`       | the workspace | The product name and the Electron version.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `chat`        | a project     | The [chat screen](#chat-screen): the messages, the waiting messages, and the composer.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `tasks`       | a project     | A placeholder.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `decisions`   | a project     | A placeholder.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `permissions` | a project     | The permission screen shell after [ADR 0015](../../docs/architecture/decisions/0015-capability-and-permission-contract-v0.md), with a placeholder section each for the agent, the repository files (`repo.read`, `repo.write`), Git (`git.commit`, `git.push`), the terminal (`terminal.execute`), the AI provider (`provider.use`), and the granted permissions. The presentation rules of ADR 0015 follow in #77.                                                                       |

- The header shows the product name, the status of the connection to the cloud API, and the local runtime status on every screen.
- The home screen's sentence while the workspace state is `signed_out` (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-06): "Sign in with GitHub to see your workspaces. The sign-in continues on the Account screen." only while the account panel offers a sign-in that can start now (`canStartSignIn` in [`src/renderer/account-view.ts`](src/renderer/account-view.ts): no sign-in runs, the panel offers one, the device key is ready, and the build has a server address), and "Workspaces appear once this device is signed in and ready." in every other case: while the device is checked at startup, the key loads or waits for its passphrase, the build has no usable server address, a removal waits for the server, or a sign-in runs, and before the first account status. `App` follows the account status for it while it is mounted. "Go to Account" shows the account screen and then moves the focus to its title (`tabIndex={-1}`), because the button leaves the page with the home screen.
- "Sign in with GitHub" on the home screen (#219, the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-06) shows only with that sentence, so by the account panel's own rule (`canStartSignIn` through `homeSignIn`), and calls the panel's main-process action, `startSignIn()` without options, through `createHomeSignInStart` in [`src/renderer/workspace-view.ts`](src/renderer/workspace-view.ts). While the start runs, the button stays focusable and does nothing (`aria-disabled`, like the buttons of the connection indicator and the device actions), so that the focus stays on it when the start is refused or fails; a `disabled` button would drop the focus to the page. The pairing code, "Use another browser", the progress, and Cancel stay on the account screen: the main process publishes the running sign-in before it opens the browser and answers the start only afterwards, so the first account status whose sign-in runs during the start shows the account screen, with the focus on its title as after "Go to Account"; the answer shows it only when no such status came, for a start that began or failed (the panel shows the failure's outcome from the status). Once per start, so the answer does not take the person back to the account screen. A refusal (`busy`, `key_not_ready`, `too_soon`) and a call that failed keep the home screen and show the panel's sentence there as an alert, until the next start, a sign-in that runs, or a change of the home screen (`homeChanged`, which `App` calls with `homeCaseForView`: the case of `homeSignIn` on the home screen, undefined on any other): the shell leaving the home screen, or another answer of `homeSignIn`, for example when the device key is no longer ready or is ready again. An answer that comes while the shell shows another screen, for example a refusal or a failed call after the status showed the account screen, shows no message. So the alert is never announced again when the home screen is shown again, and never stays next to a button that a changed status enabled. "Go to Account" stays next to the button: "Use another browser" for a person whose default browser holds another GitHub account is offered before a start only on the account screen, and the way to the account screen stays the same in both cases of the sentence.
- While a device is signed in and the account has no workspace, the home screen also offers a form to [create a workspace](#creating-a-workspace-and-a-project) (#95, D7).
- The sidebar, a `nav` landmark named "Workspace", lists the workspace screens, the account's workspaces, and the selected workspace's projects, from the [workspace state](#workspaces) of the main process (`window.desktop.workspaces`). `App` subscribes to the state while it is mounted, and every state dispatches `replace-projects`, also when the shown view stays the same, for example after a rename. A workspace is a button; the selected one is marked, and another one switches with `select`. On `too_soon`, a calm fixed text below the workspaces asks to wait a moment, and the selection stays. A switch clears the text first and then shows the text of its answer, if any; only the latest switch's answer counts, and a newer workspace state clears the text. "New workspace" below the workspaces and "New project" below the selected workspace's projects open a dialog that asks for the name ([Creating a workspace and a project](#creating-a-workspace-and-a-project)).
- Every switch goes through `switchWorkspace` ([`src/renderer/workspace-view.ts`](src/renderer/workspace-view.ts)), and every change of the open project through the report below.
- **The question before a switch** (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-05 on #27). While chat messages wait to be sent, `workspaces.select` and `openProject` change nothing and answer `{ outcome: "waiting", count }` (see [IPC contract](#ipc-contract)). This covers selecting another workspace, opening another project, and closing the project by showing a workspace screen.
  - The shell then asks the person in a modal dialog of the page ([`src/renderer/SwitchQuestion.tsx`](src/renderer/SwitchQuestion.tsx), `showModal`), never with `window.confirm`. It names the count and what the switch does. "Keep" comes first and takes the focus, Escape keeps too, and the rest of the page is inert meanwhile.
  - Keeping the messages changes nothing in the main process. The shell had already shown the other project or screen, so it goes back to the main process's open project, from the last workspace state, on its chat; without one, it shows the home screen. A workspace switch had not changed the shell.
  - Discarding them repeats the switch with `{ discardWaiting: true }`, and its answer counts like any other; a new `waiting` answer asks again. A newer switch or report replaces the question.
  - Closing the window asks too, in a native dialog of the main process (see [IPC contract](#ipc-contract)).
- The workspace logic is a plain controller, `createWorkspaceSidebar` in the same file, so that it is tested without a DOM: `start` subscribes and returns the function that ends the subscription, `openProjectChanged` reports the open project and drops the previous answer, `select` switches, and `keepWaiting` and `discardWaiting` answer the question. `App`'s `useWorkspaces` only calls it from its effects.
- Each state of the workspaces has its own fixed text: without the bridge or when the main process refuses the subscription "Workspaces are not available.", before the first state "Checking the workspaces.", `signed_out` "Workspaces appear once this device is signed in and ready." (true for every cause: no sign-in, a key that loads or waits for its passphrase, or a build without a server address), `loading` "Loading the workspaces." or, while the selected workspace loads (on the first load and after a switch), "Loading the projects.", `ready` without workspaces "No workspaces yet.", workspaces listed but none selected "Select a workspace to see its projects.", `ready` without projects "No projects yet.", and `unavailable` a fixed sentence for its problem, with the workspaces and projects that were loaded before and no "No projects yet.". The problem and the switch text share one `role="status"` paragraph below the workspaces, which is always rendered, empty when there is nothing to say, so that a screen reader announces a new text; the switch text is not shown while `signed_out`. No ID is shown. Workspace and project names are rendered as text, take their direction from their content (`dir="auto"`), and wrap when they are long.
- The shell reports its open project, or null, to the main process at the start and after every change (`openProject`), because the main process closes its open project when the page reloads. `openProject` refuses only with `not_found`, which closes the project in the shell too and shows the home screen; a failed call keeps it, and so does the answer to a report that a newer one replaced.
- A project screen adds the project navigation, a `nav` landmark named "Project" with chat, tasks, decisions, and permissions, and the side panel, an `aside` landmark with the project's agents and runs or the mounted repositories. The side panel stays as it is when another project screen or another project is shown. A mount is not linked to a project yet, so every project shows the same mounted repositories.
- The agent side panel ([`src/renderer/AgentPanel.tsx`](src/renderer/AgentPanel.tsx)) follows `window.desktop.agents` while it is shown, and changes nothing. It lists the open project's agents, each with its name, its owner's name, and for a disabled one "Disabled since" and the time. Below them, "Runs in progress" lists the unfinished runs, the most recently created first, with a note that counts the older ones the main process leaves out, and "Recently finished runs" the finished ones, the most recently finished first. The lists keep the main process's order and are never sorted. Each run shows its agent's name ("Unknown agent" for an agent that the list does not hold), its state as text, its reason, and when it was created and finished, in the person's locale and time zone. A cancelled run's reason `member_removed` or `device_revoked`, the server's cancellation codes, shows as a sentence of its own; every other reason shows as the text it is.
- Each run has its state as `data-state`, and a dot in the existing status colors: `running` running, `queued` and `awaiting_input` transition, `failed` failed, and `completed` and `cancelled` neutral. The dot only repeats the state, which is always text.
- Each state of the agent panel has its own fixed text in one `role="status"` paragraph, which is always rendered: without the bridge or when the main process refuses the subscription "Agents are not available.", before the first activity "Checking the agents.", `no_project` "Agents appear once this device is signed in and the project is open." (true for every cause), `loading` and the activity of another project, for example right after this one opened, "Loading the agents.", and `unavailable` a fixed sentence for its problem, with what was loaded before, or alone when nothing was loaded. `ready` with an empty list says "No agents yet.", "No runs in progress.", or "No finished runs yet.". No ID is shown. Names and reasons are rendered as text, take their direction from their content (`dir="auto"`), and wrap when they are long.
- The panel's logic is in [`src/renderer/agent-view.ts`](src/renderer/agent-view.ts), so that it is tested without a DOM: `followAgentActivity` subscribes, and `describeAgentPanel` turns a view into the texts and lists that `AgentPanelContent` renders without state.
- The shown screen is marked with `aria-current="page"`, and the selected workspace and the open project with `aria-current="true"`. Every navigation item is a button, so the keyboard reaches all of them, and a control focused with the keyboard shows a focus outline.
- The side panel is chosen with tabs (`role="tablist"`, `aria-selected`). Only the selected tab is in the tab order; the arrow keys, Home, and End select another tab and move the focus to it.
- The actions are `show-screen`, `open-project`, `show-panel`, and `replace-projects`. The reducer refuses a project screen or a side panel while no project is open, an unknown project, and an unknown screen, panel, or action, and then returns the same state object. A first view that is not valid, for example one whose project does not exist, shows the home screen. Two projects with the same ID are refused, by `createShellState` and by `replace-projects`, which also validates the shown view again, so a view whose project is gone shows the home screen. Projects that equal the shown ones return the same state object.

The styling is a placeholder. [`src/renderer/styles.css`](src/renderer/styles.css) reads colors, fonts, spacing, radii, borders, and the column widths from custom properties on `:root`; colors are written nowhere else. The theme follows the system setting (`prefers-color-scheme`), and a `data-theme` attribute of `light` or `dark` on the root element overrides it, so that the visual design can add a theme setting. The selected item has a background, a bold label, and a bar in the accent color, and in forced colors an underline. The visual design (#122) replaces these tokens and adds the base components. The Content Security Policy allows no inline styles, so styling stays in the stylesheet and never uses a `style` attribute.

### Chat screen

The chat screen ([`src/renderer/ChatScreen.tsx`](src/renderer/ChatScreen.tsx); #27) shows the open project's chat from `window.desktop.chat` ([IPC contract](#ipc-contract)). The main process holds the chat and sends the messages; the screen shows its state and passes the person's actions on. Each project gets its own screen, so a draft never moves to another project.

- **Structure.** `ChatScreen` is a thin hook around the pure `ChatScreenContent`. The logic is in [`chat-view.ts`](src/renderer/chat-view.ts): `createChatScreen` subscribes and carries out the actions, and `describeChatScreen` turns a view into texts and lists. The tests drive both without a DOM.
- **States.** One `role="status"` paragraph, always rendered, has a fixed text for each state:
  - no bridge or a refused subscription, and before the first state;
  - `no_project`, which is also the state without a signed-in device;
  - `loading`, and a chat of another project, so that another project's messages never appear;
  - `unavailable` with a fixed sentence per problem, with what was loaded before; with nothing loaded, only the problem shows;
  - `ready` without messages shows "No messages yet.".
- **Messages.** The oldest first, each with its author's name, its kind ("Person" or "Agent"), and its time in a `<time>` element, in the person's locale and time zone. A body is rendered as text with `dir="auto"`, and keeps its line breaks (`white-space: pre-wrap`).
- **Waiting messages.** Below the messages, in the order written: "Waiting for the connection" and Discard, "Sending…" without a button, and "Not sent" with a fixed sentence for the reason, Retry, and Discard. Each button is described by its message's body. The next state shows what followed.
- **Older messages.** "Load older messages" while `available`; while `loading` it stays focusable and does nothing (`aria-disabled`). `none` says "This is the start of the chat." and `not_shown` that older messages are not shown. Why the last older page did not load and the answer `too_soon` share one status paragraph; a newer state clears the answer.
- **Left out.** Notes count the older messages and the later waiting messages that the view leaves out (`moreMessages`, `morePending`).
- **Composer.** A text area with the visible label "Message" and a hint. Enter sends, and Shift+Enter adds a line; an Enter that ends an input method's composition only ends it.
  - It is disabled while the shown project is not the main process's open project, which is also the case without a signed-in device.
  - The text goes to `send` as it is, and stays read-only until the answer. It is cleared only when the send is accepted. A text longer than the bridge takes (64,000 characters, `CHAT_LIMITS.sendText`) shows the `too_long` sentence at once, because the bridge would refuse it before the main process sees it; a failed call keeps "The message could not be sent. Try again.".
  - A refusal keeps the text and shows a fixed sentence for its reason, which the next change of the text clears.

### Sign-in renewal

The account panel shows when the device's sign-in ends and offers to renew it ([`src/renderer/AccountPanel.tsx`](src/renderer/AccountPanel.tsx) and [`account-view.ts`](src/renderer/account-view.ts); #95, decision D4 of 2026-11-06). A sign-in lasts 30 days (ADR 0014, "Lifetimes"); the main process passes its end on in the account status ([Account](#account), "Signed in"), and a renewal is a GitHub sign-in of the device that is signed in ([Account](#account), "Renewal").

- **When it shows.** Only while this installation's device is signed in (`deviceSignedIn`: the status names the account, and the device state is `signed_in`). In every other state the panel says what the device needs, as before; a hidden account (#195) names no end.
- **The end.** Below the GitHub login and the name, "Signed in until" and the end as a date and a time of day in the person's locale and time zone (`signInEndFormat`: `dateStyle: "long"` and `timeStyle: "short"`, for example "9 December 2026 at 10:00"; the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-09), in a `<time>` element with the UTC time.
- **Renew at any time.** "Renew the sign-in" is always offered while the device is signed in, no sign-in runs, the device key is ready, and the build has a server address (`offersRenewal`), as the first button of the panel's actions, before "Sign out". It is the same button at any time, not a smaller link before day 25: one control, in one place, that a keyboard reaches like the others, and a renewal before day 25 does no harm. It calls the panel's existing start (`startSignIn()` in the default browser), so the main process applies the cooldown, `busy`, and the key checks as to any other start, and the panel shows the sign-in's progress, its pairing code, "Use another browser", and its outcome as for any sign-in. While an action of the panel runs, the button is disabled like the others.
- **From day 25.** From day 25 of the 30 days, which begins after 24 days, so 6 days before the end (`SIGN_IN_RENEWAL_WINDOW_MS`, the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-09), a fixed sentence above the actions asks the person to renew: "The sign-in of this device ends soon. Renew it to keep using this device." After the end by the renderer's clock, while the device is still signed in, because the server has not refused it yet (an open connection may go on for up to 24 hours), it says "The sign-in of this device has ended. Renew it to keep using this device." The sentence uses the warning style of the registration lock line, has no live role, and describes the button (`aria-describedby`).
- **After the end.** Once the server refuses the device with `sign_in_required`, the device state changes, the end and the renewal go away, and the existing texts apply: the panel says "The server asks for a new GitHub sign-in on this device." and offers "Sign in with GitHub", and the connection header says "Sign in with GitHub on the Account screen to connect again.". "Renew" is used only while the device is signed in, and "Sign in" once the server asks for it.
- **Clock.** `describeSignInExpiry(status, now)` and `nextSignInExpiryChange(status, now)` are pure. The panel reads the renderer's clock again when day 25 begins and when the sign-in ends (the same watch as the retry note of #199, which reads the clock again when its timer fires), and when the window gets the focus or becomes visible again, as the devices section does, because a timer can fire late after the computer slept. So the request to renew appears without a restart while the Account screen stays open.
- **A refused renewal.** A renewal in a browser that holds another GitHub account is refused with `device_account_mismatch`, and the device stays signed in ([Account](#account), "Renewal"). The panel then shows the outcome, "This installation belongs to the GitHub account octocat. Sign in with that account.", and keeps the end and "Renew the sign-in"; it never asks to remove the device, which only the `account_mismatch` state of a device that was not signed in does.
- **Styles.** No new rule: the end is one more fact of `account-facts`, the sentence uses `account-warning`, and the button `panel-action`.

### Devices section

The devices section ([`src/renderer/DevicesSection.tsx`](src/renderer/DevicesSection.tsx); #95, decisions D1, D4, and D5b of 2026-11-06) sits on the Account screen beside the account panel, headed "Devices". It shows the [device list](#device-list-service) of the main process from `window.desktop.devices` ([IPC contract](#ipc-contract)), and offers the device actions: remove another device, revoke another device as lost, and lift the registration lock. The main process confirms each in a native dialog ([Account](#account), "Other devices").

- **Structure.** `DevicesSection` is a thin hook around the pure `DevicesSectionContent`. The logic is in [`device-view.ts`](src/renderer/device-view.ts): `followDeviceList` subscribes and asks the main process to load the list once when the section opens (`devices.load()`), also before a device is signed in, which the main process counts as the request, `describeDeviceList` turns a view into texts and lists by a given clock, and `createDeviceActions` runs the device actions and keeps their sentence; whether the advice shows comes from the main process's list. The tests drive them without a DOM.
- **States.** One `role="status"` paragraph, always rendered, has a fixed text: "Devices are not available." without the bridge or when the main process refuses the subscription, "Checking the devices." before the first state, "The devices appear once this device is signed in." for `signed_out`, "Loading the devices." while the list loads, "No devices are listed." for an empty list, and a fixed sentence for each problem of `unavailable`, with what was loaded before, or alone when nothing was loaded.
- **Devices.** The active devices first, under "Active devices", then the revoked ones apart, under "Revoked devices", each group in the main process's order (the most recently created first). This device is marked with "This device" as text and `aria-current="true"`. Each device shows its label as text with `dir="auto"`, its platform, and when it was created, last seen ("Never connected" while `lastSeenAt` is null), last signed in, and when its sign-in expires; a revoked device shows when it was revoked instead of the expiry. A device created in the last 7 days (`NEW_DEVICE_WINDOW_MS`) is marked "New". Times are `<time>` elements in the person's locale and time zone, as the chat screen shows them.
- **Registration lock (D5b).** While `registrationLockedUntil` is later than the renderer's clock, a line says that new devices cannot be registered on this account until that time, and "Lift the registration lock" follows it, described by the line. It shows only while the lock is in force by this clock, so never with an ended lock or without one; the main process accepts the lift whenever its last list reported a lock (see [Device list service](#device-list-service)).
- **New devices (D4).** Each notice says that a new device was registered on this account, with its label as text, its platform, and when. The notices sit in one polite log region (`role="log"`, named "New devices"). The region is rendered only once the section has the first state of the main process, so it enters the page together with the notices that this state holds; it then stays, also empty, for every later state. A live region announces changes to its content after it is in the page, not the content that it enters with, so the notices that were there when the screen opened are not read out, and a notice that a later state adds is. "Dismiss the notices", outside the region, asks the main process to end the notices that the section shows, by their device IDs (`dismissNewDevices`), so that a notice that arrived after the last state that the section received stays (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-09). Once the main process answers that it ended a notice, the focus moves to the section's title, which takes it without a tab stop (`tabIndex={-1}`), because the button goes away with the notices; a call that fails or ends nothing keeps the focus on the button. Each notice has "This was not me: revoke as lost", bound to its device ID and described by the notice (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-09). It sits in the notice, inside the region, so a screen reader reads it with a notice that is added. The main process accepts it also while its list does not hold that device yet, for example while the list fails to load or before the reload after the frame.
- **Device actions (D1).** Each other active device has "Remove" and "Revoke as lost", described by its label, which takes an ID by its place in the list, never the device ID; this device has "Remove this device" in the panel instead, and a revoked device has none. "Remove" revokes the device as `removed`, without a lock; "Revoke as lost" and the notice's action revoke it as `lost`, and the native dialog says before the person confirms that this starts the 7-day registration lock, during which no new device can register. While an action runs, every device action's button stays focusable and does nothing (`aria-disabled="true"` and no click handler, as in #211), so that the native dialog gives the focus back to it; the dismissals still work. The sentence of the last action sits in its own `role="status"` paragraph, always rendered with the bridge, empty when there is nothing to say: a fixed sentence for every result, refusal, and failure ("The device could not be revoked." or "The registration lock could not be lifted." followed by the reason), and nothing after a kept confirmation. An answer that does not show whether the server acted (`unknown`) says so, and that the request is not sent again on its own. After an action whose button leaves the page with the next list (a revocation, a lift), the focus moves to the section's title, but only while the focus is still on that button or on the page (`focusesTitleAfterAction`). The status paragraph itself never takes the focus, so a screen reader reads the sentence once (the review of #228 noted that a focused live region can be read twice; the Account screen's title and the dismissals of this section use the same title). A new action clears the sentence, and so does a drop of the list: a list that shows no signed-in device, one that loads again after a list that had loaded or failed, or one that names another device as this one (`listChanged`), so that a sentence about one account never stays for another. An action whose answer comes after such a drop shows no sentence. The list's first load for a device keeps the sentence of an action that ran before it, such as a notice's action. The actions follow each view of the list in the same call that passes it to the section (`followDeviceList`), so the sentence goes in the same render as the list that ends it.
- **Advice after a lost device (ADR 0014).** After a revocation as lost, and also when its answer does not show whether the server revoked the device, because the person confirmed that the device is lost, the section shows "After a lost device" with three fixed sentences: end that device's GitHub sessions at github.com/settings/sessions (the address is text, not a link that opens on its own), change the GitHub password if the device was unlocked, and rotate the AI provider credentials stored on that device, which the server cannot reach. The main process holds it as `lostDeviceAdvice` of the device list (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-09), so it shows again after the person leaves the Account screen or the page reloads, until "Dismiss the advice" ends it through `devices.dismissLostDeviceAdvice()`; a sign-out or another account or device drops it. Once the main process answers that it ended the advice, the focus moves to the section's title; a call that fails or ends nothing keeps the focus on the button.
- **Clock.** The section reads the renderer's clock for every new view, again when a device stops counting as new or the lock ends (`nextDeviceListChange`), and when the window gets the focus or becomes visible again, because a timer can fire late after the computer slept, so that the marks and the lock line follow the time while the screen stays open.
- **Styles.** The section reuses the account panel's styles and tokens; the new rules (`device-name`, `device-label`, `device-mark`, `device-notices`, `device-notice`, `device-advice`, and an inert `panel-action` with the muted text color and the progress cursor) use tokens only.

### People screen

The people screen ([`src/renderer/PeopleScreen.tsx`](src/renderer/PeopleScreen.tsx); #95, decisions D1 to D3) shows the selected workspace's people from `window.desktop.people` ([IPC contract](#ipc-contract)). The [workspace service](#workspaces) holds them and keeps them current with the membership events; the screen follows them while it is shown. It offers the people actions that the account's role allows through `window.desktop.peopleActions` ([People actions](#people-actions)); the list changes only with the people that the main process sends once the API's event arrives, never from an action's answer. For the owner and the admins it also shows the open invitations and offers the invitation actions through `window.desktop.invitations` and `window.desktop.invitationActions` ([Invitations](#invitations)).

- **Structure.** `PeopleScreen` is a thin hook around the pure `PeopleScreenContent`. The logic is in [`people-view.ts`](src/renderer/people-view.ts): `followPeople` subscribes, and `describePeopleScreen` turns a view into the texts, the lists, and whether the hint shows. The actions are apart, in [`people-actions-view.ts`](src/renderer/people-actions-view.ts) and [`PeopleActions.tsx`](src/renderer/PeopleActions.tsx): `describePeopleActions` turns a view and the renderer's clock into the actions that the screen offers, and `createPeopleActions` runs them, one at a time, and keeps the open question, the name field, and the sentence of the last action. The invitations are apart as well, in [`invitations-view.ts`](src/renderer/invitations-view.ts) and [`InvitationActions.tsx`](src/renderer/InvitationActions.tsx): `followInvitations` subscribes and asks for the list once, `describeInvitations` turns the people, the invitations, and the renderer's clock into what the screen shows of them, and `createInvitationActions` runs the invitation actions, one at a time, and keeps the open question, the code's dialog, and the sentence of the last invitation action. The tests drive the logic and the controllers without a DOM; the hooks that connect them to React are not tested (see [Tests](#tests)).
- **Heading.** The heading stays "People", a fixed landmark, and takes the focus without a tab stop (`tabIndex={-1}`) after an action whose control leaves the page. Below it, "Workspace:" and the selected workspace's name, as text with `dir="auto"`, whenever the main process names one: the name from the workspace list, the same as the sidebar's.
- **States.** One `role="status"` paragraph, always rendered, has a fixed text for each state: "People are not available." without the bridge or when the main process refuses the subscription, "Checking the people." before the first state, `signed_out` "People appear once this device is signed in and ready.", `no_workspace` "No workspace is selected.", which is true both without any workspace and without a selected one, `loading` "Loading the people.", `ready` without an active person "No people yet.", and `unavailable` a fixed sentence for its problem, with the people loaded before; with nothing loaded, only the problem shows.
- **People.** The active people come first, sorted by the screen (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-09): the owner, the admins, the claimed members, then the unclaimed people, each group in the main process's order. That order is the server's from the last reload, with the people that events added since at the end; an event moves nobody until the next reload, but a role change or a claim moves the person to its new group on the screen at once. Each shows its display name as text with `dir="auto"`, its role ("Owner", "Admin", or "Member"), and its GitHub login in the monospace font with the date it was last verified, in a `<time>` element in the person's locale and time zone, so that a display name cannot pass for a login (ADR 0014, "People and roles"). An unclaimed person shows "Not claimed yet" instead of a login. The account's own person has "(you)" after the name. No ID is shown.
- **Former members.** Removed people follow under "Former members", their own list, in the main process's order, without a role, with the login they had; one whose account's data was erased shows "No GitHub login", and one that no account ever claimed shows "Never claimed", because a removed person can no longer be claimed (ADR 0014). The group shows only when it has someone. Their last role is a later option.
- **Left out.** When the main process sends 1,000 people and leaves the rest out, a note after the last list counts them.
- **Admin hint.** Only when the owner can act on it (#95 asks to encourage at least one admin besides the owner; the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-09): the account's own person is the active owner, no active person is an admin, and at least one active, claimed person other than the owner could become one, because an unclaimed person cannot be promoted. The text is fixed: "Only you can manage this workspace. Consider making someone you trust an admin, so they can also invite people and answer join requests." Admins and members never see it. It does not show while the main process leaves people out (`morePeople` above 0), because an admin could be among them. The screen cannot see the server's own limit either: the view's answer holds at most 1,000 people, the active, claimed ones (admins among them) before the unclaimed and former ones, so the server leaves an admin out only when the workspace has more than 1,000 active, claimed people, and the hint could then show although an admin exists.
- **Who sees which action.** By the roles table of ADR 0014 ("People and roles"), only while the people are `ready` and the account's own active person is listed; the server enforces the table, and the screen offers only what it allows. The owner and the admins have "Add person" (a name field below the active people) and "Rename" and "Remove" on each unclaimed person; the owner also has "Remove" on every other claimed person, an admin only on a claimed member. Only the owner has "Make admin" on a claimed member, "Make member" on an admin, and "Offer ownership" on every other claimed person while no offer is live. The owner sees the live offer, with its target's name and its end, and "Withdraw the offer"; its target sees "You are offered the ownership of this workspace until" its end, with "Accept the ownership". Neither sees the offer once its `expiresAt` has passed by the renderer's clock, which is read again at that time and when the window gets the focus or becomes visible. The admins and the members have "Leave this workspace"; the owner cannot leave. A former member has no action, and the account's own person has none in its row.
- **Questions.** Every destructive action and every role change except "Make admin", that is remove, make member, leave, offer ownership, and accept the ownership, asks first in a modal dialog of the page (`showModal`), like the question before a switch: its title, the person's or the workspace's name as text with `dir="auto"`, a fixed sentence about what follows, "Cancel" first and then the confirming button. The name, under the fixed ID `people-dialog-name`, describes the dialog before the sentence (`aria-describedby`), so that a screen reader says whom the question is about. The sentences of a removal of a claimed person and of a leave say what ADR 0014 and the server do: the agents in the workspace's projects are disabled, the permissions on them revoked, the unfinished runs cancelled, and the open invitations that the person created revoked. Escape cancels. "Rename" opens the same dialog with a name field that holds the current name; a name that is empty after trimming or longer than 64 characters (code points, as the contract and the protocol's zod `.max` count them, so 64 emoji fit), keeps the dialog open with a fixed text in an alert inside it, because the page behind it is inert. Neither name field sets `maxLength`, which HTML counts in UTF-16 code units, so this check alone decides the length. "Make admin" and "Withdraw the offer" run at once. A question ends when what it shows changes: a question that the screen no longer offers exactly as it was asked, for example after the person went away, was claimed or renamed, the people stopped being `ready`, another workspace was selected, or the offer expired, ends in the controller as well (`listChanged` with the view and the clock), so it never opens again by itself and a later confirmation sends nothing; a rename's draft is lost with it, so that no question shows an outdated name. Closing returns the focus to the action's button; when that button has left the page, the focus moves to the screen's title.
- **One action at a time.** While an action runs, every action's button and "Add person" stay focusable and do nothing (`aria-disabled="true"` and no click handler, as in the devices section), and nothing else starts. This holds across the people actions and the invitation actions: while one of either kind runs, the controls of both kinds, "Refresh the invitations" included, are inert. After an action whose control leaves the page with the next people (all but a rename and a definition) and that the API confirmed, the focus moves to the screen's title while it is still on that control or on the page.
- **Results.** The sentence of the last action sits in its own `role="status"` paragraph, always rendered with the bridge, empty when there is nothing to say: a fixed sentence for each confirmed action; for a refusal, the action's start ("The person could not be removed.") and a fixed text for each reason, with the wait in seconds, minutes, or hours for `too_soon` and `rate_limited`; and for `unknown`, that it is not known whether the action happened, to check the list (the workspace list after a leave), and that the request is not sent again on its own. Nothing is ever sent again on its own. For a definition, `unclaimed_limit` says that the workspace already has as many unclaimed people as it allows, and to remove one or invite one of them, without a wait, because none helps (#242); `rate_limited` always asks to wait. A call that fails on its way says that nothing was sent. An answer counts for its workspace only when no switch, also none away and back, and no sign-out happened while it was on its way (a selection generation); otherwise it shows no sentence, moves no focus, and leaves a name typed since alone. Only a confirmed leave is apart, because its membership event can switch the workspace before its answer arrives: it keeps its sentence while the same session is signed in, and in that session lets the focus move to the title, because its button goes with its workspace; after a sign-out it moves no focus. A definition sends the trimmed name and empties the field only once the API confirmed it; an empty or too long name is not sent, and the field's own alert (`define-person-problem`) says why until the name is edited; a rename's problem ends the same way. The field is described by its hint and that alert, not by the results. Another selected workspace drops the question, the name field, and the sentence; a sign-out always does, also when no workspace was selected before. The sentence of a confirmed leave stays through the switch that the leave brings, but only when its answer arrives while the left workspace is still selected; a later switch ends it.
- **Invitations: who sees them.** Only the owner and the admins, by the roles table of ADR 0014 ("Create and revoke invitations; list live invitations with their creators"), and only while the people are `ready` and the account's own active person is listed, as for the people actions; the main process also holds the list for them only. Members see no invitations part and no "Invite". Without both bridges the screen has none of it either.
- **The open invitations (D3).** Below the people, under "Open invitations", before "Leave this workspace". Each live invitation shows the invited person's display name from the people ("A person who is not shown here" when the people do not list it), "Created by" and its creator's name, both as text with `dir="auto"`, and "Expires" with its end as a date and a time in the person's locale and time zone (the offer's format, `offerTimeFormat`), in a `<time>` element. The list keeps the main process's order, holds only the selected workspace's invitations (a state of another workspace shows "Loading the invitations." and no list), and leaves out an invitation past its end by the renderer's clock, which is read again at the soonest end and when the window gets the focus or becomes visible. One `role="status"` paragraph of the part, always rendered, has a fixed text: "Invitations are not available." without the subscription, "Checking the invitations." before the first state and while the main process does not list them for this role yet, "Loading the invitations.", "No open invitations.", and a fixed sentence for each problem of `unavailable`, with what was loaded before. When the screen opens, it subscribes and asks the main process to load the list once (`invitations.load()`, D3); from then on the main process reloads it after its own triggers. "Refresh the invitations" asks again; its answer shows in the invitation actions' status paragraph: "Loading the invitations again." until the next state of the invitations arrives, or a fixed sentence for `too_soon`, `not_signed_in`, `no_workspace`, and `not_allowed`.
- **Invite and Revoke.** "Invite" is the first button on each active unclaimed person's row and is described by the person's name; "Revoke" is on each open invitation and is described by the invited person's name, whose element ID is the invitation's place in the list, never its ID. Both ask first in a modal dialog built like the people actions' question, with its own element IDs: the title, the person's name as text, a fixed sentence, "Cancel" first, and then "Create the invitation" or "Revoke". The question of a create says that the code works once, within 7 days, the API's default, because the screen sends no lifetime; that the code is shown only once; and that a person's earlier open invitation is revoked and its code stops working (ADR 0014, "Invitations"). A question ends when the screen no longer offers it as it was asked, for example after a claim, a rename, the end of the invitation, or another workspace, as for the people actions. Each request carries the workspace ID that the screen showed, so the main process answers `not_selected` for a workspace selected since; a create sends the member ID and a revocation the invitation ID. Each is sent once and never again on its own.
- **The code (D2).** After a confirmed create, the code shows once in a modal dialog: "Invitation code", the person's name as text, the sentence "This code is shown only now. Once you close this, it cannot be shown again. Pass it on privately, only to this person, for example in a direct message: anyone who has the code can claim this person.", the code in a read-only field (`readOnly`, monospace, `dir="ltr"`, `translate="no"`, no `name`, and in no form), which gets it only as its `value` property, set through a reference in a layout effect when the dialog opens and emptied in the effect's cleanup when it closes (`showCodeInField`), never as a `value` attribute, so the markup never holds the code, the end ("It can be used once, until" and the time), "Select the code", and "Close". "Select the code" focuses the field and selects its text, and touches nothing else: the renderer never uses the clipboard, so the person copies the selection with the system's own command. The controller holds the code only in the view of the open dialog: "Close", Escape, and a close by the browser drop it, and so do a switch of the selected workspace and a sign-out; the dialog then renders empty, and no later view, status paragraph, attribute, or log holds the code. An answer that arrives after a switch, also one away and back, or after a sign-out, drops the code unseen, as for the people actions. After a switch, a confirmed create's workspace says, once it is shown again (at once when the answer comes after a switch away and back), "An invitation was created, but its code could not be shown. Invite the person again for a new code." (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-10); no other workspace says it, the sentence holds no code, and the controller keeps only that workspace's ID for it. The next action and a refresh end it, also before it shows, and once shown a switch ends it like every sentence; after a sign-out it never shows. No channel returns the code again: after `unknown`, the sentence says to check the list and that a new invitation, which revokes an earlier one, is the way to a code. While the dialog is open, no other invitation action starts. After it closes, the dialog returns the focus to "Invite", or to the screen's title when that button left the page.
- **Results of the invitation actions.** In their own `role="status"` paragraph (`invitation-action-message`) right after the people actions' one, always rendered with the bridges, empty when there is nothing to say: "The invitation was created." or "The invitation was revoked."; for a refusal, "The invitation could not be created." or "The invitation could not be revoked." and a fixed text for each reason (`not_found` and `conflict` with a text of their own for each action, and the wait in seconds, minutes, or hours for `too_soon` and `rate_limited`); for `unknown`, that it is not known whether it happened, to check the open invitations, and that it is not sent again on its own; and for a call that fails on its way, that nothing was sent. A create's `rate_limited` names the limit of 50 live invitations (ADR 0014, "Limits"; `MAX_LIVE_INVITATIONS`, the renderer's copy of the protocol's `INVITATION_COUNT_LIMITS.live`) only when the latest list shows at least 50 live invitations of other people, because the person's own one is replaced and does not count; otherwise it asks to wait, as the API's limit of 100 new invitations in 24 hours also answers `rate_limited`. After a confirmed revocation, the focus moves to the screen's title while it is still on "Revoke" or on the page, because the row leaves with the next list. A switch or a sign-out drops the question, the code, and the sentence.
- **Not yet.** A note says that answering join requests will be added here; renaming the account's own person and choosing an invitation's lifetime are later options.

### Join screen

The join screen ([`src/renderer/JoinScreen.tsx`](src/renderer/JoinScreen.tsx); #95, decision D2) lets a person whom a workspace already added join it with an invitation code, through `window.desktop.invitationActions` ([Invitations](#invitations)): it previews the pasted code, and accepts it after a question. The request to join by a workspace ID and the account's own join requests follow.

- **Structure.** `JoinScreen` is a thin hook around the pure `JoinScreenContent`. The logic is in [`join-invitation-view.ts`](src/renderer/join-invitation-view.ts): `createJoinInvitation` runs the preview and the accept, one at a time, and keeps the field's text, its problem, the preview, the question, the sentence of the last request, and the joined workspace; `describeJoined` and `joinAvailability` turn the workspace state into what the screen offers. `App` passes the workspace state that the sidebar follows and the sidebar's switch. The tests drive the logic and the controller without a DOM; the hook is not tested (see [Tests](#tests)).
- **Texts.** The heading "Join a workspace" takes the focus without a tab stop (`tabIndex={-1}`) after an action whose control leaves the page. Under "Join with an invitation code", a fixed hint says that a workspace that already added the person can give them an invitation code for that person, as ADR 0014 asks the join screen to say ("Join requests"), that the workspace and the person show before the person joins, and that the application takes a code only when it is pasted there, never from a link (P-59). Without the bridge, "Joining with an invitation code is not available." replaces the hint and the form; while the workspace state is `signed_out`, "You can join a workspace once this device is signed in and ready." replaces the form. A note says that asking to join by a workspace ID and the own join requests with their withdrawal come later.
- **The field.** A text field labelled "Invitation code", described by the hint and by its own alert, which says why a text was not sent. It takes no spelling check, completion, capitalization, correction, or translation (`spellCheck={false}`, `autoComplete`, `autoCapitalize`, and `autoCorrect` off, and `translate="no"`), because the code is a credential. "Preview" trims the text, as the Project Manager decided on 2026-11-06 (#212; the main process trims nothing), and checks it with `INVITATION_CODE_PATTERN`, a copy of the protocol's pattern that the tests compare with the protocol's and with the contract's `InvitationCodeTextSchema`. The trim (`pastedCode`) works at both ends only: it removes what `trim` removes, the format characters (`\p{Cf}`, such as a zero-width space, joiner, or non-joiner, a word joiner, a byte order mark, and the marks of the text's direction, which chat applications add), and the next line character U+0085. A character inside the text stays, so a text that is not a code never becomes one: the pattern is anchored and accepts ASCII only. The trim walks inward from both ends by index, a character at a time, and never copies the text into an array, so a very large paste costs only the characters that it removes. Nothing is sent for an empty text ("Paste an invitation code.") or a text that is not a code ("This is not an invitation code. A code starts with mmdi_ and has 48 characters. Paste all of it."). Editing the text ends its problem and its preview.
- **The preview.** The workspace's name; "You join as" and the name of the person that the code claims; "Invited by" with the inviter's name, the GitHub login in the monospace font, and the date it was last verified; and "Valid until" with the expiry's date and time, in the person's locale and time zone. Names and the login are text with `dir="auto"`. A fixed sentence asks to check the inviter's login, not only the name, and "Join the workspace" follows, described by the facts. Once the renderer's clock reaches the expiry, "Join the workspace" and the sentence about the login go, an open question closes, nothing is sent, and the status paragraph says "The invitation has expired. Ask the person who sent it for a new one."; the facts stay. The controller reads the clock at the expiry (`nextJoinClockChange`), when the window gets the focus or becomes visible, before it opens the question, and before it sends an accept; a preview whose answer arrives when the clock has already passed the expiry keeps "Join the workspace" and is not ended by the clock: the server found the invitation live as it answered, so the clock runs ahead, and the server decides on the accept.
- **The question.** "Join the workspace" asks first, in a modal dialog of the page (`showModal`) like the people actions' questions, because joining has effects that ADR 0014 tells the person about ("People and roles", "Invitations"). It shows the workspace's name and "You join as" with the person's name, as text, and a fixed sentence: the account claims the person and becomes a member, everyone in the workspace sees the account's GitHub login next to the person's name, the owner and the admins are told of the claim with the login and the creation date of the GitHub account, and the code stops working. "Back" comes first and keeps the code and the preview, and Escape goes back too; "Join" accepts.
- **The code (D2).** It lives only in the field's text and in a request on its way: no sentence, problem, preview, or log holds it, and a call that fails is reported with a fixed text, never with its error. It leaves the field as "Join" sends it, whatever the answer, so a refused accept, also `rate_limited` and `too_soon`, needs the code pasted again (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-10). "Cancel", which shows while there is a text, a problem, or a preview, drops the code with its problem, its preview, and the last sentence. A change of the selected workspace away from one drops the code with its problem, its preview, the question, and a preview's sentence. The first selection after none, such as the default workspace that the workspace service selects once the list loads, keeps them, because a preview and an accept are not tied to the selection. A sign-out drops everything. Leaving the screen drops the controller with the code. A preview's answer counts only while no "Cancel", switch away from a workspace, or sign-out happened since; an accept's answer counts in the same session, also after a switch, because the workspace service may select the joined workspace by itself.
- **One request at a time.** While a preview or an accept runs, the field is read-only, and "Preview", "Cancel", and "Join the workspace" stay focusable and do nothing (`aria-disabled="true"` and no click handler). Each request is sent once and never again on its own.
- **Results.** One `role="status"` paragraph, always rendered with the bridge, holds a fixed sentence: after a preview, "The invitation is shown below. Check it before you join."; after an accept, "You joined the workspace."; for a refusal, the request's start ("The invitation could not be shown." or "You did not join the workspace.") and a fixed text for each reason: `invitation_invalid` (the code may be mistyped, expired, revoked, or used, or its person removed; ask for a new invitation), `already_member` (the code was not used; tell the sender, so that they can revoke it), `rate_limited` and `too_soon` with the wait in seconds, minutes, or hours, `not_signed_in`, `unavailable`, `invalid`, and `failed`; for `unknown`, that the preview got no clear answer, or that it is not known whether the account joined, with a request to check the list of workspaces and that nothing is sent again on its own; and for a call that fails on its way, that it was not sent.
- **After an accept.** Of the answer, only the joined workspace's ID and name are kept, and the ID only reaches the switch; the answer holds no user ID (D3). The main process loads the workspace list again, and the workspace service selects the joined workspace only when none was selected. The screen names the joined workspace as text and offers "Open this workspace", which selects it through the sidebar's switch, while the list holds it and it is not selected; otherwise it says that the workspace is selected now, or that it appears once the list has loaded. A new preview ends it.
- **Focus.** "Cancel" moves the focus to the title, because it leaves the page. When "Join the workspace" goes because the preview ends with an accept's answer, a switch, or a sign-out, or because the invitation expired, and when "Open this workspace" goes because the workspace is selected, the focus moves to the title if it fell to the page. Closing the question returns the focus to "Join the workspace".
- **Styles.** The screen uses the existing classes of the account panel and the people screen, and adds none.

### Creating a workspace and a project

The shell creates a workspace and a project (#95, decision D7) through `window.desktop.workspaces.create` and `createProject` ([Creates](#creates)). The logic is a plain controller, `createCreation` in [`src/renderer/creation-view.ts`](src/renderer/creation-view.ts), so that it is tested without a DOM; the parts that render are in [`src/renderer/Creation.tsx`](src/renderer/Creation.tsx), and `App` holds one controller for the whole shell.

- **Where.**
  - The home screen shows "Create a workspace", a form with a name field and "Create", while a device is signed in, the list loaded, and the account has no workspace.
  - In the sidebar, "New workspace" follows the workspaces whenever a device is signed in, also while the list loads, so that it does not leave the page with every switch.
  - "New project" follows the selected workspace's projects whenever a workspace is selected. Any active member of a workspace may create a project in it (ADR 0005, update of 2026-11-02, #155), and the list holds only the workspaces of which the account is an active member, so every role gets it, and the screen reads no role.
  - Each opens a modal dialog of the page (`showModal`), built like the people screen's rename: the name field comes first and takes the focus, Escape cancels, "Cancel" comes before "Create", and closing the dialog returns the focus to the button.
- **No question.** "Create" creates at once. The form and the dialog say first what a create does:
  - for a workspace (ADR 0014, "People and roles"), that the person becomes its owner, that its people see them under the account's display name, and that an owner cannot leave without handing the ownership over first;
  - for a project, that everyone in the workspace sees it, and that two projects of a workspace cannot have the same name, whatever the case.
  - A project's dialog names its workspace, from the list, as text.
- **Names.** They follow the protocol's rule. A name is trimmed before it is sent, and a project's name is normalized to Unicode NFC first, as the protocol normalizes it (`normalizeProjectName`); a workspace's name is not normalized, as in the protocol. An empty name, or one over 100 characters, is caught with a problem in its own alert and not sent. The length counts characters (code points), as the protocol counts them (#251), and only when the name is longer in UTF-16 code units. Neither field sets `maxLength`, which HTML counts in UTF-16 code units. Control, format, and invisible characters are refused by the main process with a fixed sentence.
- **One create at a time.** While a create runs, "Create" on the home screen, "New workspace", and "New project" stay focusable and do nothing (`aria-disabled="true"` and no click handler), and nothing opens. The dialog closes as its create starts, so the focus returns to its button. Each create is sent once and never again on its own.
- **The workspace that was shown.** A project's dialog keeps the selected workspace's ID from when its button was shown, and sends it; the main process refuses another workspace as `not_selected`.
- **Results.** Each place has its own `role="status"` paragraph with a fixed sentence, rendered with the bridge whether or not it has something to say: below "New workspace", below "New project" while a workspace is selected, and on the home screen.
  - A workspace: "The workspace was created and selected.", or, when the main process did not select it, "The workspace was created. Select it in the list of workspaces to open it.", which is "The workspace was created." next to "Open this workspace" (see below).
  - A project: "The project was created. It shows in the list of projects once the server reports it.", which is "The project was created." next to "Open this project", once the list holds it (see below).
  - A refusal: "The workspace could not be created." or "The project could not be created.", and a fixed text for the reason. `too_soon` and `rate_limited` say how long to wait, in seconds, minutes, or hours. `rate_limited` also names the API's limit that it answers with: 10 new workspaces per account in 24 hours (ADR 0014, "Limits"), or 20 new projects per account in 24 hours (#155). `project_limit`, the limit of 100 projects per workspace, names no wait, because none helps (#255): it says that the workspace already has as many projects as it allows, that projects cannot be removed yet, and to create the project in another workspace. `conflict` says that a project of the workspace already has the name.
  - `unknown`: it is not known whether it was created; the person checks the list before creating it again, and nothing sends it again on its own. A call that fails on its way: it was not sent.
- **What a sentence belongs to.**
  - A project's answer counts only while its workspace has stayed selected since the create started; a switch, also away and back, drops its sentence and its name.
  - A workspace's sentence stays through the switch to the workspace that it selected, in either order of the state and the answer, and ends with any other switch.
  - The home screen's sentence shows only on the home screen that started the create; leaving the screen ends it and the field's problem.
  - A sign-out ends the dialog, the names, the problems, and the sentence, and no answer of a create on its way counts after it.
  - A cancelled or refused dialog keeps its name for the next time it opens; a confirmed create empties it.
- **Opening what was created** (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-12). The sentence of a confirmed create keeps the new workspace's or project's ID, never in the markup, and `describeCreatedOffer` turns it and the latest workspace state into at most one button after the status paragraph at the sentence's place. `App` gives the controller the existing paths (`createdOpenPaths`), and `openCreated` takes the one that the state offers at the click.
  - **A new workspace that stays unselected**, because chat messages wait to be sent or the selection changed while the create was on its way: "Open this workspace", below "New workspace" or on the home screen, while the list holds it and it is not the selected workspace, like the join screen's button (#249). It selects the workspace through the sidebar's switch (`WorkspaceSidebar.onSelect`), which asks "discard the waiting messages and switch?" while chat messages wait (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-05); the button never asks or discards on its own. Once the workspace is selected, its sentence ends with the switch, as before.
  - **A new project** never opens by itself. Once its `workspace.project.created` event lists it, and only while its workspace is still the selected one and it is not the open project, "Open this project" follows its sentence below "New project". It opens the project as a click in the list of projects does: the shell's `open-project`, which `App` reports to the main process, whose chat service asks first while chat messages wait.
  - **Texts.** The labels are fixed, without a name or ID, and each button is described by its sentence (`aria-describedby` on the status paragraph's fixed ID, `create-status-home`, `create-status-workspaces`, or `create-status-projects`).
  - **The sentence next to a button** (the Project Manager's decision for #263). While a button follows its sentence, the sentence is the short one, "The workspace was created." or "The project was created."; before the button shows, the long sentence stays, which says what to do without it. Once the button has shown, the sentence stays short until it ends, also after the button left, for example once the project is open, so that it never grows back: the controller marks the sentence `offered` once a state offers its button, also when the state comes before the answer, and `describeCreateSentence` gives the sentence in two parts. The status paragraph is a polite, atomic live region (`role="status"`); the short sentence is the start of the long one, and the rest of the long one is a text node of its own, so the sentence shortens by removing that node alone, which should not be announced under ARIA's default `aria-relevant` (additions and text); this was not checked with a screen reader. The create is meant to be announced once, and its button stays described by the same paragraph.
  - **One action at a time.** A create that starts ends the sentence with its button, so no button is shown and nothing opens while it runs. The buttons are built like the other create controls: one shown while a create runs would stay focusable with `aria-disabled="true"` and do nothing.
  - The creator's display name and the limit of unclaimed people stay out of the creates, as before.
- **Focus.** The home screen's form leaves the page once the new workspace is listed, so after a confirmed create there the focus moves to the home screen's title, which takes it without a tab stop (`tabIndex={-1}`), while the focus is still in the form or on the page. When the dialog ends after its button left the page, for example with a sign-out, the focus moves to the sidebar's "Workspaces" heading, which takes it the same way. When "Open this workspace" or "Open this project" leaves the page while the focus fell to the page, for example once the workspace is selected or the project open, the focus moves to the heading above it (`focusAfterOpenLeaves`): the home screen's title for the home screen's "Open this workspace", the "Workspaces" heading for the sidebar's, and the sidebar's "Projects" heading for "Open this project" (the Project Manager's decision for #263), which takes it without a tab stop too, or the "Workspaces" heading while the "Projects" heading is not on the page, for example once no workspace is selected.
- **Names as text.** Every name renders as text with `dir="auto"`: the fields, the workspace's name in a project's dialog, and the lists. No ID appears in the markup.
- **Styles.** The dialog and the form use the people screen's classes; `.nav-create` places the sidebar's buttons, and the home screen's "Open this workspace" uses `.account-actions`.
