When the header button that had the focus goes away with its action, for example once the removal is confirmed and the status becomes `offline`, or after "Reconnect here once", the status paragraph takes the focus without scrolling (`focusesStatus`, `tabIndex={-1}`, `preventScroll`), so it does not drop to the page. The buttons note when they take the focus and when they lose it while still in the page and while the window has the focus; Chromium fires no blur when it removes the focused button. The native dialog of the removal deactivates the window, and the button blurs then, but it stays the page's focused element, so the note stays, whether the status removes the buttons before the window comes back or Chromium focuses the button again first. A change that the person did not start from a header button, such as `waiting` becoming `connecting` after the backoff or a sign-out on the Account screen, moves no focus, and neither does a change while the focus is elsewhere.
- **Secrets.** No status, result, or log holds a frame, a nonce, a signature, a connection ID, a user ID, or a key. The client's log messages are fixed text with a close code at most; the close reason's text, which may name a network error, is read only for 1008 and never logged. The signed frame goes only to the socket.
- **Known limits.** `net.WebSocket` answers the server's pings itself and shows none of them, so a connection that died without a close is noticed only when TCP or the system reports it, or at the next system resume; a protocol-level heartbeat is a later protocol change. A proxy that asks for Basic or Digest credentials is not prompted for them, the same as `net.fetch` today; cached credentials are used. A handshake that the API refused with `403` or `429` arrives as 1006, so it backs off like a dropped connection.
- **Manual test.** A connection to a locally running API, and one connection through a system proxy (PAC or a manual proxy), are part of the later manual test and are not recorded here. The proxy record holds the operating system and the Electron version, the proxy setting, the output of `session.resolveProxy` for the https form of the API URL, the proxy's `CONNECT` log line, the desktop status after `auth.ok`, and the API's log line of the authenticated connection. Chromium never sends `127.0.0.1` or `localhost` through a proxy unless the bypass list contains `<-loopback>`, so that record needs that setting or a hosted HTTPS API (#96).

Not in this part: the chat UI (M2-03) and agent activity (M2-04); presence or events beyond the status; event replay; a prompt for Basic or Digest proxy credentials; a protocol-level heartbeat; packaging (#17); and the API's `Origin` change, which #154 made.

The tests ([`client.test.ts`](src/main/connection/client.test.ts), [`connection-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/connection-ipc.test.ts), [`connection-status.test.ts`](src/renderer/connection-status.test.ts), and the connection tests in [`account.test.ts`](src/main/account.test.ts), [`App.test.tsx`](src/renderer/App.test.tsx), [`ipc-contract.test.ts`](src/shared/ipc-contract.test.ts), and [`bridge.test.ts`](src/preload/bridge.test.ts)) use a fake socket factory, a fake account service, and injected timers and clocks. They cover the authentication sequence (nothing sent before the challenge, one signed frame, `auth.ok` of the signed device), the start and the stop with the account (sign-out, removal, revocation, quit, another device, and a change of the same device that opens nothing), one socket at a time with the wait for the close event, `update_required` from the challenge and from 1008, both timers, every close reason with its status and the device ID passed to `deviceRefused`, the end of `connection_replaced` and `protocol_violation` with a new status when the device is removed or signs out (and not on a change of the same device), their return without a socket when the same device is signed in again without a new sign-in (after a removal that the API refused, twice in a row, with the reconnect still working; after each way of losing the device; and after a sign-in that was cancelled or failed), a new sign-in of the same or another device after a sign-out, during a pending removal, or after the removal's token request needed a sign-in, with the device reported while the sign-in runs or only at its end, the end of the remembered stop after a reconnect, a new sign-in, or another device, a sign-in that succeeds while the stop holds and the device stays signed in (it connects; one that was cancelled or failed, or an earlier `signed_in` outcome, keeps the stop), a sign-in that already ran when the stop came, also when the device leaves during it, and a sign-in that ended one stop, which does not count for the next one, the refusals and `update_required` that stay across a sign-out, the single reconnect after `key_replaced`, the backoff bounds for every attempt with the lowest and the highest random value, its reset after 60 seconds connected and not before, the resume, binary frames, invalid JSON, protocol refusals, frames out of order, a message over 1 MiB in characters and in UTF-8 bytes, `sendAs` as the only way to send an event (no `build` before `auth.ok` of the signed device, the identity of that `auth.ok` in `build` and in `identity()`, its clearing from an authenticated socket on a close, a replacement, another account, a sign-out, and the quit, a send after the socket changed, a `build` that throws, replaces the socket, or returns what JSON cannot carry, an event of another device or account, an agent actor's event from the identity's device, and the send guards: buffered data, size, and the protocol check), the resubscription after every `auth.ok`, the message listeners, the reconnect, its refusals, and its budget, the re-serialized sends, the account service's signature with a pending replacement and its refusals with the stop of local execution, the status schema and the reconnect result, the IPC handlers (foreign senders, arguments, unknown keys dropped, and events to subscribers), the bridge, and the indicator with its buttons: the actions of every state and reason, the actions of `connection_replaced` only for a signed-in device (every other device state, account, and an unknown account status), the offline sentence for each case of `canStartSignIn` and its neutral form while the register-again sentence shows, the removal's single call while it runs with both buttons inert, the sequence from `connection_replaced` through the removal to `offline` for a confirmed and for a pending removal, a pending message that follows the device after "Remove this device" on the Account screen, also when the device ends `revoked`, a refused removal whose message stays next to the restored `connection_replaced` and both buttons, and on a connection, until a sign-in, the next removal, or a reconnect from the header, both reconnect buttons calling the removal's `reconnecting` before the reconnect, no button after a sign-out or "This is not me", the focus rule (no move after an automatic change, a change without a focused header button, or a focus elsewhere) and the buttons' focus notes, also across the blur when the removal's native dialog opens, the message of each result, and no removal without the account bridge. They also check that every status fits the contract and that no status or log holds the nonce, the signature, the connection ID, a device or user ID, or a frame, and that no file of the client logs with `console`.

## Agent activity

[`src/main/agents/activity.ts`](src/main/agents/activity.ts) holds the agent activity of one project (M2-04, #28): its agents from `GET /projects/:projectId/agents`, and its unfinished and recently finished runs from `GET /projects/:projectId/runs`, kept current with `agent.run.state_changed` events. It is a pure model, with no timers, no I/O, and no logging. Its functions return new frozen objects and never change their arguments: `activityFromSnapshot` reads both answers, `replaceAgents` takes a new answer of the agent route alone, and `applyRunEvent` applies one event.

- **Snapshot.** Every agent and every run must belong to the project in the path; otherwise the answers are refused as `wrong_project`. The server's order is kept and never sorted: the unfinished runs the most recently created first, the finished runs the most recently finished first. The model keeps 50 finished runs (`RECENT_FINISHED_RUNS`), or as many as the server sent when that is more.
- **The last event wins.** After `workspace.subscribed`, the buffered events are applied in order on top of the reload, which can be newer than some of them. An event sets the state and the reason that it reports; its `previousState` is never compared with the held state. An event that reports what the model already holds changes nothing (`unchanged`), so a repeated relay is harmless. A `repositoryId` in an event never changes the run's repository.
- **A finished run never changes.** An event for a run in the finished list is older than the snapshot and changes nothing.
- **Finishing and creation.** A terminal state moves the run to the front of the finished list, with the event's `occurredAt` as a provisional finish time that the next reload replaces with the server's; the oldest finished run beyond the limit is dropped. A creation (`previousState` null) of a run that the model does not hold adds it at the front of the unfinished list.
- **Reloads.** An event that changes a run of a listed agent that the model does not hold, or a held run of another agent, is `unknown_run`, and the caller reloads. An event for a run that the model does not hold, of an agent that the project does not list, is `unknown_agent`: most often its run belongs to another project of the workspace, because the event names no project, and the caller does not reload for it. An event for a held run applies also when the agent list does not hold the run's agent, for example an agent created between the two reads of the snapshot: the snapshot showed that the run belongs to this project. A server cancellation with a code of `RUN_CANCEL_REASONS` (`device_revoked` or `member_removed`) sets `agentsStale` whatever the outcome, because the revocation or removal also disabled agents and no event reports that; the caller then reloads the agents.

The [agent activity service](#agent-activity-service) uses it for the open project, the [development agent switch](#development-agent-switch) reads that service, and the agent side panel shows it ([Screens](#screens)) (#28).

The tests ([`activity.test.ts`](src/main/agents/activity.test.ts)) build every answer through the protocol's schemas and every event through `decodeServerMessage`, as the connection client receives it, and freeze them. They cover the copy and the freeze of answers that are not frozen, the project check of both answers and of a new agent list, empty lists, the server's order, the finished limit (50, or the larger count of the snapshot) with the drop of the oldest finished run, an update in place that leaves its input unchanged, a reason that is replaced or cleared, the move to the finished list with the provisional finish time, events for finished runs, buffered events over a newer snapshot, unknown agents and runs, a held run of another agent, the events of a held run of an agent that the list does not hold, a creation at the front with and without a repository and with a reason, the stale agents after a server cancellation (also when the runs already show it, and for a run of another project), a repository in an event, and a repeated event.

## Workspace resync

The resync gate ([`src/main/workspace/resync.ts`](src/main/workspace/resync.ts); #27, #28) applies the protocol's rule for `workspace.subscribed` (the protocol package's [README](../../packages/protocol/README.md#workspace-subscriptions-presence-and-chat), decision 5): the device reloads the workspace over HTTP, buffers the workspace's events until the reload answers, and then applies them in order on top of the reloaded state. It is generic over the event type, does no I/O, and keeps no timers. Each service that holds workspace state (the workspace, chat, and agent services) owns one gate, so their reloads stay independent; the [workspace service](#workspaces), the [agent activity service](#agent-activity-service), and the [chat service](#chat-service) use one each.

- **Generations.** Every reload starts a new generation before its request is sent: `subscribed` after each `workspace.subscribed`, and `begin` for the others (a change of the open project, a reload that the service decides on, a retry, and the reload after an overflow). `reloaded(generation)` returns the buffered events in order and goes live; for a stale generation, or one that already answered, it returns `null` and the service drops the answer, for example one for the previous project. `reloadFailed(generation)` keeps the events buffered until the retry. `begin` during a reload keeps the buffered events, since applying an event again changes nothing. `generation()` lets a service drop the late answer of another request, such as an older page of the chat.
- **Scope.** A reload reads `all` of a service's state or a `part` of it, such as the agent list alone. `subscribed` always reads `all`. `begin(scope)` returns the generation and the scope that the service then requests: the scope asked for, or `all` while an earlier reload has not answered (it is open, it failed, or an overflow or `end` made it stale), because its dropped answer may have covered any part of the state. So a part reload never replaces the reload after `workspace.subscribed`, and `live` means that the whole state was reloaded since `subscribed`.
- **Events.** `push` returns `apply` while live, `buffered` during a reload, and `ignored` without a subscription. A reload begun without a subscription only gets a generation; the gate goes live only after `subscribed`.
- **Limits.** At most 1,000 events (`MAX_BUFFERED_EVENTS`) and 4 MiB (`MAX_BUFFERED_EVENT_BYTES`, measured with the service's `sizeOf`) wait for a reload. 4 MiB holds 64 of the largest chat messages and 4 of the largest messages that the connection client accepts, while 1,000 of the largest chat messages would hold about 64 MB in each gate. Above either limit, the buffer is cleared (`overflowed`), the open reload becomes stale, and later events are dropped until the service calls `begin`, whose reload then reads `all`. The gate never starts a reload itself, so the service's backoff decides when. Dropping is safe because the server relays an event only after its commit: a reload that begins after an event arrived contains it.
- **End.** `end`, for the end of the subscription, a change of the workspace, or a lost connection, drops the buffered events and changes the generation, so that the open reload and every request noted with `generation()` become stale. It does so also without a subscription, so that the answer of a reload for the previous workspace is dropped; a service that still needs that reload begins it again.

The tests ([`resync.test.ts`](src/main/workspace/resync.test.ts)) cover events without a subscription, while buffering, and while live; the order of the returned events; a second `workspace.subscribed` and a second `begin` during a reload; a failed reload and its retry; a stale failure; the overflow by count, by bytes, at the budget, with an invalid size, and after a failure; that the size is measured only for buffered events; the scope of a part reload while live, without a subscription, and after an open, a failed, an overflowed, or an ended reload; `end` in every state; a reload without a subscription; and two independent gates.

## Chat timeline

The chat timeline ([`src/main/chat/timeline.ts`](src/main/chat/timeline.ts); #27) is the model of one project's chat in the main process: the messages that the desktop holds, the oldest first, and the person's own messages that the server has not stored yet. It is pure: no I/O, no timers, and no IPC, and every result is frozen. The [chat service](#chat-service) calls it.

- **Body.** `prepareMessageBody` turns a carriage return, alone or before a line feed, into a line feed, and then applies the protocol's rules for a message body (`multilineText`): not blank, at most 16,000 code points (`LIMITS.messageBody`), and no other control, format, or invisible character.
- **Messages.** Each message ID and each `seq` is held once, in `seq` order. `applyNewestPage` replaces the held messages, so a reload never leaves a gap; held messages newer than the page stay, and so do the pending copies. While older pages exist, a newest page that ends below the held messages with a gap is stale, for example the answer to an earlier request that a later reload overtook. A reload that changes nothing returns the same timeline. `applyOlderPage` adds an older page only when the lowest held `seq` is still the `beforeSeq` that was sent, and drops it as stale otherwise. Both check what the schema cannot see: every message belongs to the project, the page holds at most the `limit` that was sent, and every `seq` of an older page is below its `beforeSeq`. `hasOlder` is true after a full page that does not reach `seq` 1; the server numbers a project's messages from 1 without gaps. `applyCreated` inserts a relayed message by `seq`, unless it is already held or lies below the held messages while older pages exist, and ignores another workspace or project.
- **Pending copies.** A timeline belongs to an account, a device, a workspace, and a project (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-05 on #27), and so do its pending copies. A copy is `waiting` for the connection, `sending`, or `refused` with the code of `event.refused`. `chat.message.created` removes the copy whose event ID is its `causationId`. `pendingForResend` lists the waiting copies only for the timeline's own key, and `keepForIdentity` drops the timeline after a sign-out or a change of account or device. A timeline holds at most 50 copies (`MAX_PENDING_CHAT_MESSAGES`). The retries after `rate_limited` and their timing belong to the [chat service](#chat-service).

The tests ([`timeline.test.ts`](src/main/chat/timeline.test.ts)) cover the body rules (line breaks, blank text, the limit counted with astral characters, and control, format, and invisible characters, written as escapes), duplicates, relays out of `seq` order, the page checks, `hasOlder`, stale older and newest pages, a reload that changes nothing, a reload that keeps the pending copies, the pending copy replaced through `causationId`, a duplicate created event, a refusal, the key checks, the limit on pending copies, and that every result is frozen and no argument changes.

## Workspaces

The workspace service ([`src/main/workspace/service.ts`](src/main/workspace/service.ts); #27, #28) holds the account's workspaces, the selected workspace's people and projects, and the open project in the main process, read with the account service's [workspace reads](#account) and kept current with the workspace's events. It sends no write: creating a workspace or a project goes through the account service ([Creates](#creates)), which adds a confirmed workspace with `addWorkspace`; a new project comes with `workspace.project.created`. [`index.ts`](src/main/index.ts) creates it after the [connection](#connection) and closes it at quit (`will-quit`). The renderer's sidebar ([Screens](#screens)) reads its IDs and names and selects a workspace or opens a project over IPC ([`src/main/workspace-ipc.ts`](src/main/workspace-ipc.ts); see [IPC contract](#ipc-contract)), and the [people screen](#people-screen) reads the selected workspace's people over their own channels ([`src/main/people-ipc.ts`](src/main/people-ipc.ts)); the chat and agent services and the development switch read it in the main process.

- **List.** `GET /workspaces` loads whenever a signed-in device (the same condition under which the connection connects) reaches `connected`, because account frames sent while it was offline are lost. One load runs at a time; a trigger during a load drops its answer and loads again, so only an answer that was asked for after the last trigger applies. A failed load is retried after min(60 s, 2 s x 2^n), where n is the number of failures in a row before this one (2 s after the first); a `rate_limited` failure waits 60 s (`RATE_LIMITED_RETRY_MS`) and still counts toward n. A retry is scheduled only while connected: a lost connection cancels the retry, a load that fails while offline schedules none, and the next `connected` loads at once. A sign-out, or another device, resets everything and drops the answers on their way.
- **Selection.** Kept in memory only (decided on #27 on 2026-11-05). It defaults to the first workspace of the list, the most recently claimed, and stays while the list holds it. `select(workspaceId)` takes a workspace of the list, unsubscribes the previous one through the connection client, and subscribes the new one, so the client's set holds the selected workspace only. The open project closes with it.
- **Reload.** After every `workspace.subscribed` of the selected workspace, the service reloads its view (`GET /workspaces/:workspaceId`) and its projects through its own [resync gate](#workspace-resync), and applies the events that arrived meanwhile in order on top of the answers. Answers for another workspace are refused as `failed`. A failed reload is retried with the same rule as the list (min(60 s, 2 s x 2^n), where n is the number of failures in a row before this one, 2 s after the first; a `rate_limited` failure waits 60 s and still counts toward n), while the events keep waiting. A successful reload starts the count again. `not_found` also reloads the list. An overflow of the gate's buffer reloads after the same backoff and counts toward n. A lost connection ends the subscription until the next `workspace.subscribed`.
- **Events.** `workspace.member.defined`, `claimed`, `renamed`, `removed`, and `role_changed`, `workspace.ownership.transferred`, and `workspace.project.created` and `renamed` of the selected workspace. Each sets the state that it reports, so a repeated event changes nothing. They go through the service's [resync gate](#workspace-resync) like the reload: during a reload they wait and apply after it, in order, so none is lost, and since each person is held once by member ID, an event that the reload already holds adds no person twice. A live event that names a person or a project that the service does not hold reloads the workspace and changes nothing; after a reload, such an event is skipped. `defined` adds a person that the service does not hold, at the end of the people, and `claimed` sets the whole person from the event, in place for a held unclaimed person or at the end for one that the service does not hold. The replay rule (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-09): `defined` is the first event of a person's life, and a claim happens once, so `defined` for a person that the service holds, and `claimed` for a person held as claimed, can only be replays, for example events that waited during a reload whose answer is newer, and they change nothing. A replay therefore never undoes a removal, a role change, a rename, a newer verification of the login, or the erasure of a former member's account; `renamed` sets the display name, `role_changed` the role, `removed` the removal time, and `workspace.ownership.transferred` makes the previous owner an admin and the target the owner. A removed person stays as a former member with their name and role.
- **Membership.** After `workspace.subscribe_refused`, `workspace.unsubscribed` with `membership_ended`, and `account.removed_from_workspace`, the service calls the connection client's `unsubscribeWorkspace`, which otherwise sends the subscription again after every `auth.ok`, drops the workspace from the held list, selects the first remaining workspace or none, and reloads the list. A refused workspace is not selected by default while the lists hold it, so a list that still holds it does not subscribe to it in a loop; the person can still select it. Every list answer that applies forgets the refused workspaces that it does not hold, so a later listing, a new membership, is selected by default again. `account.join_request_decided` with `approved` forgets that the workspace was refused and reloads the list.
- **People.** The selected workspace's people (#95, decision D3), from the view's `members`, which hold every person, unclaimed, active, and former: each as `{ id, displayName, role, claimed, identity, removedAt }`, where `claimed` says whether an account claimed the person, `identity` is `{ login, verifiedAt }` of that GitHub account, null while unclaimed and for a former member whose account's data was erased, and `removedAt` is null while the person is active. The service never keeps the user ID. `people()` gives them as a frozen list, in the order of the last reload, which is the server's, with the people that events added since at the end; it is empty until the selected workspace's view loaded. The people are dropped with the rest of the workspace's data on a workspace switch, at the end of the membership (`account.removed_from_workspace`, `workspace.unsubscribed` with `membership_ended`, and `workspace.subscribe_refused`), on a sign-out, and for another device; a lost connection keeps them until the next reload. The invitations and the join requests of the view are not kept; their own services read them ([Invitations](#invitations), [Join requests](#join-requests)).
- **Ownership offer, limit, and pause (#95, D3).** The view's live ownership offer, as `{ memberId, expiresAt }` (`ownershipOffer()`, null without one), its limit of active unclaimed people (`unclaimedMemberLimit()`), and the time when its join requests were paused (`joinRequestsPausedAt()`, null while they are accepted), from each reload, dropped with the rest of the workspace's data. No event reports a pause or its end: the [join request actions](#join-requests) reload the view after a pause and a resume, and the other devices see it at their next reload. No workspace event reports an offer or its end: the API tells the target's account and the owner's account (#241), with `account.ownership_offered` and `account.ownership_offer_ended`, and no other member. Those frames for the selected workspace reload it on either side, rather than set the offer from the frame, so that an older reload answer never undoes them. `workspace.ownership.transferred` to the offer's target and `workspace.member.removed` of the target end the held offer at once, because an offer names an active person who is not the owner. A removed person never becomes active again, so a replayed removal never ends a newer offer; a replayed transfer can, when the ownership went to the target and back and the target got a new offer while one reload was open, and the next reload shows the offer again. The owner's offer and withdrawal also reload the workspace from the [people actions](#people-actions), so the acting device reads it even when its frame was lost while it was offline; its frame and that reload merge as below. The owner's other devices reload on the frames. An offer past its `expiresAt` stays held until the next reload; the people view hides it.
- **Reload on request.** `reload(workspaceId)` reads the selected workspace's view and projects again through the resync gate, as a reload after `workspace.subscribed` does: the events that arrive meanwhile wait and apply after it. Requests are merged and keep the backoff: while a reload is open, however many requests arrive, one more reload follows its answer, which may be older than the change; while a failed reload or an overflow waits out its backoff, also the 60 seconds after `rate_limited`, nothing is read and the retry reads the change. The offer frames go through it too, so repeated offers and withdrawals cannot stack reloads, and after `rate_limited` they wait out its 60 seconds; frames that come further apart than one reload's round trip each start a reload. Until the follow-up reload answers, the answer of the open reload, which may be older than the change, shows for a moment. It answers false, and reads nothing, for another workspace and while the workspace is not subscribed, because the next `workspace.subscribed` reloads it anyway.
- **State.** `{ revision, status, problem?, workspaces, selectedWorkspaceId, projects, openProjectId }`, frozen, with `onChange` listeners. `status` is `signed_out`, `loading` until the list and the selected workspace loaded once, `ready`, or `unavailable` with the failure as `problem` while a retry waits, or until the next connection after a failure while offline; what was loaded before stays. `workspaces` holds `{ id, name }` in the server's order, and `projects` holds `{ id, name, createdByMemberId }`, the oldest first. `openProject(projectId)` opens a project of the selected workspace only, `openProject(null)` closes it, and a project that a reload no longer lists closes. The revision also grows when only `memberName`, `people`, or `gateLive` would answer differently.
- **For the chat and agent services.** `memberName(memberId)` gives a person's display name, also of a former member, and `Unknown person` (`UNKNOWN_MEMBER_NAME`) for a person that the selected workspace does not hold. `gateLive(workspaceId)` is true only for the selected workspace while it is subscribed and reloaded with no reload open; those services and the development switch send events for a workspace only then. `selectedWorkspaceId()` gives the selected workspace. `ownMemberId(workspaceId)` gives the account's own person in a listed workspace, the `member` of the list's answer, and null for a workspace that the list does not hold; an ended membership and a sign-out forget it. The development switch checks an agent's owner with it, and the people IPC marks the account's own person.
- **A created workspace (#95, D7).** No account frame or workspace event reports a new workspace, so `addWorkspace(workspace, ownMemberId)` puts a workspace that the account created first in the list at once, from the API's confirmed answer, because the list holds the most recently claimed first and the creator's person is claimed at the create; a workspace that the list holds already keeps its place. It keeps the account's own person in it for `ownMemberId`, and loads the list again: a load on its way may have read before the create, so its answer is dropped, as for any trigger. It does not select the workspace. False, with nothing changed, without a signed-in device or after `close`.
- **Logs.** Fixed text with a failure code at most, never a name or an ID.

Not in this part: presence (`workspace.subscribed`'s `onlineMemberIds` and `presence.changed` are ignored, because no issue owns showing it yet), the workspace's name from its view, and the invitations and join requests, which have services of their own.

The tests ([`service.test.ts`](src/main/workspace/service.test.ts)) use a fake account service with reads that the test answers, a fake connection client with the client's set of requested workspaces, and injected timers, and build every frame and event through `decodeServerMessage`. They cover the load after `connected`, also for a service created while the client is connected, and not without a signed-in device with a ready key, the selection with exactly one subscription, the reload after every `workspace.subscribed` and the dropped answer of an earlier one, events during the reload applied after it and buffered events for an unknown person or project skipped without another reload, the project and member events while live and the reload for an unknown person or project, which changes no person (so a transfer to an unknown person demotes no owner), the people of the view (unclaimed, claimed, and former, one whose account's data was erased among them) as a frozen list without any user ID, every membership event while live with its effect on the people and a repeated event that changes nothing, not even the revision, membership events during a reload applied after it over an older answer and over an answer that already holds them, each person once, a reload that replaces the people with its answer, in its order and without the people held before, the replay rule (a claim of a person held as claimed that changes nothing, whatever its login or verification; a claim of an unclaimed person, live, that sets the whole person, also of a person that the service does not hold; a claim that waited during a reload whose answer holds the person removed, with no published state that shows the person active, also before the live removal arrives; a claim that keeps the role of the answer, also live; a definition that keeps the removal of a person never claimed; a definition and a claim that keep the identity of a newer answer; and a claim that never shows the login of an erased account again), a transfer from an unknown person that promotes nobody, the people dropped on a switch, the end of the membership, a sign-out, and another device, and kept while offline, events of another workspace, runs, and presence, projects of another workspace, the refused subscription (also of the only workspace, without a loop, and again after an approved join request) and a refused workspace selected again after a list without it, `membership_ended`, `requested` for the selected and for another workspace, the listeners' notice when the gate goes down, `account.removed_from_workspace`, a trigger during a list load, the approved and the denied join request, the backoff of the list and of the reload with 60 seconds after `rate_limited` and its new start after a success, no list retry while offline, the overflow, the sign-out and another device, also while a list load is open, a renewal of the sign-in, also after an attempt refused for another GitHub account, that keeps the state, the subscription, and the open project, the open project, `ownMemberId` (also after an ended membership, a new list, a sign-out, and `close`), the ownership offer and the limit of unclaimed people from the view, dropped on a switch, the reload after the offer frames of the selected workspace only, `reload` through the gate and nothing for another workspace, while not subscribed, or after `close`, requests merged into one more reload after an open one and left to a waiting retry, also after `rate_limited` and after an overflow, also one asked before the overflow, a replayed removal of the offer's target that keeps a newer offer to load on its way dropped, a listed workspace kept in its place, and nothing without a device or after `close`), and that no log holds a name or an ID and the file does not log with `console`.

The IPC tests ([`workspace-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/workspace-ipc.test.ts), and the workspace tests in [`ipc-contract.test.ts`](src/shared/ipc-contract.test.ts) and [`bridge.test.ts`](src/preload/bridge.test.ts)) cover the foreign senders, every refused argument (not a UUIDv7, another UUID version, uppercase, a wrong type, a missing or an extra argument, and a string far over the length) before the service is asked, the protocol's check of the actions without the contract, the contract's ID schema against the protocol's, the limits against the protocol, the frozen copy without member IDs, the caps on the lists and the names, the state sent to subscribed frames only, the budget of switches with the real service (a loop of alternating selects reaches the connection client only within the budget, and a switch works again after the window), a refused close, the question before the window closes while chat messages wait (kept, closed, asked once at a time, the window brought to the front and restored, a quit before the question and one during it, after "Close" and after "Keep", a failed question, and a window destroyed meanwhile), the close of the open project on the window's close, a new document in the main frame, a failed load of the main frame, and a gone render process with the selection kept, and none on the start of a load, an aborted load, or a subframe, and the bridge's functions.

## People actions

The people actions of #95 ([`src/main/people-actions-ipc.ts`](src/main/people-actions-ipc.ts)) are nine writes of the [allowlist](#account) in the main process, each behind its own strict channel (decision D1): define a person, rename, remove, promote, demote, leave, offer the ownership, withdraw the offer, and accept it. The renderer reaches them through `window.desktop.peopleActions` ([IPC contract](#ipc-contract)).

- **Checks.** The contract refuses a value that is not a lowercase UUIDv7 or a display name of 1 to 64 characters, a missing argument, and an extra one, before anything runs. The main process then checks each ID again with the protocol's `WorkspaceIdSchema` and `MemberIdSchema` (`invalid`), and refuses a workspace that is not the selected one as `not_selected`, so that a renderer cannot act on another workspace. The account service checks the display name with the protocol's request schema, which also refuses a blank name and control, format, and invisible characters (`invalid`). Nothing is sent after a refusal.
- **Sent once.** Each action is one `sendWorkspaceAction` of the account service, which takes care of the budget of 30 writes a minute, the pause while an action of the person or a sign-in runs, and the session. Nothing sends it again: an uncertain answer, and one that throws, is the outcome `unknown`, and the screen asks the person to check the list.
- **Results.** `ok`, for a definition with the new person's member ID only, never its user ID; a definition whose answer names another workspace is `unknown`. A failure maps to one fixed refusal (`PERSON_ACTION_REFUSALS`): `invalid`, `too_soon`, `not_signed_in`, and `unavailable` as the account service answers them; the API's `forbidden` as `not_allowed`, and `not_found`, `conflict`, `device_too_new`, `unclaimed_limit`, and `rate_limited` as they are, which are the codes that the person routes answer (`MEMBERSHIP_ROUTE_ERRORS`); the codes of invitations, join requests, and unknown workspaces, and any other refusal, as `failed`. `too_soon` and `rate_limited` carry `retryAfterMs`. A definition answers `unclaimed_limit`, without a wait, when the workspace holds as many active unclaimed people as its limit (#242). The API answers a withdrawal or an acceptance without a live offer, also of an expired one, with `conflict`; `not_found` names an unknown workspace or person.
- **The people afterwards.** An action changes nothing in the main process. The API sends the membership event of each confirmed action (`workspace.member.defined`, `renamed`, `removed`, `role_changed`, and `workspace.ownership.transferred`) to every connection subscribed to the workspace, this device's included, and the [workspace service](#workspaces) applies it like any other event, so an answer and its event never both change the people, in whichever order they arrive. Only an offer and its withdrawal have no event for the owner: after either is confirmed, and after any of the three offer actions that answers `unknown`, `not_found`, or `conflict`, the service reloads the workspace (`reload`), so that the shown offer is current.
- **Leave.** After a confirmed leave, the API ends this device's subscription with `workspace.unsubscribed` and `membership_ended` and sends `account.removed_from_workspace`; the workspace service then drops the workspace, selects the first remaining one, and reloads the list ([Workspaces](#workspaces), "Membership"). The owner cannot leave (`not_allowed`).

The tests ([`people-actions-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/people-actions-ipc.test.ts)) use a fake account service that builds each request with the real allowlist, and once the real workspace service. They cover the sender check and every argument refusal before anything is sent, the protocol's check of the IDs again, a blank name or one with a line feed or an invisible character answered `invalid` for a definition and a rename without a request, `not_selected` for another workspace or none, each action's exact route, path parameters, and body, every failure's result, each code of the person routes mapped to its own refusal, the definition's member ID without a user ID, one request after an uncertain answer and after one that throws, with the fixed report for a thrown answer only, the reload after a thrown answer for the three offer actions only, `too_soon` and `rate_limited` with their wait, the reloads after the offer actions only, the people changed only by the event of a confirmed action in either order, the offer that the owner sees after a reload, and a leave followed by the frames of the membership's end.

## Creates

Creating a workspace and a project (#95, decisions D1 and D7; [`src/main/creation-ipc.ts`](src/main/creation-ipc.ts)) are two writes of the [allowlist](#account) in the main process, `createWorkspace` and `createProject` (#212), each behind its own strict channel. [`index.ts`](src/main/index.ts) registers them with the account service, the workspace service, and the chat service's switch.

- **Checks.** The contract refuses a name that is not 1 to 100 characters, counted in code points (`NewNameSchema`, `WORKSPACE_LIMITS.name`), an ID that is not a lowercase UUIDv7, a missing argument, and an extra one, before anything runs. A project's workspace ID is checked again with the protocol's `WorkspaceIdSchema` (`invalid`), and another workspace than the selected one is refused as `not_selected` before anything is sent. The account service checks the body with the protocol's request schema: a blank name, or one with control, format, or invisible characters, is `invalid`, and a project's name goes as the schema normalized it.
- **The display name.** A workspace takes the account's display name from the account status as the creator's person, its owner, as the protocol asks (`CreateWorkspaceRequestSchema`; ADR 0014, "People and roles"); without a signed-in account, nothing is sent (`not_signed_in`). No limit of unclaimed people is sent, so the API applies its 500.
- **Sent once.** Each create is one `sendWorkspaceAction` of the account service, with its budget of 30 writes a minute and its pause while an action of the person or a sign-in runs. Nothing sends it again: an uncertain answer, and one that throws, is `unknown`; a throw is reported with a fixed message, never the error.
- **Results.** A failure maps to one fixed refusal, checked with `satisfies`: for a workspace `CREATE_WORKSPACE_REFUSALS` (`invalid`, `not_signed_in`, `unavailable`, `too_soon`, `rate_limited`, `failed`), for a project `CREATE_PROJECT_REFUSALS`, which adds `not_selected`, `project_limit`, `not_found`, and `conflict`. The API's limits pass through as they are: 10 new workspaces per account in 24 hours and 20 new projects per account in 24 hours answer `rate_limited` with `retryAfterMs`, and 100 projects per workspace answers `project_limit` without a wait, because no time frees a place (#255). Every other code that the API does not answer on the route is `failed`.
- **A new workspace.** The answer holds the workspace and the account's own person in it. The main process adds the workspace to the workspace service's list at once (`addWorkspace`, [Workspaces](#workspaces)), which also loads the list again, because no account frame or workspace event reports it. It then selects it through the chat service's switch, with the waiting chat messages kept: only while the selection is the one that the create began with, so a workspace that the person selected meanwhile stays selected, and only while no chat message waits to be sent, so none is dropped. The selection subscribes to the workspace, and its `workspace.subscribed` loads its people and projects. The answer is `ok` with the new workspace's ID and `selected`; the person's member ID and user ID stay in the main process.
- **A new project.** The answer is `ok` with the new project's ID only; a project of another workspace is `unknown`. Nothing changes the projects here: the API sends `workspace.project.created` to every connection subscribed to the workspace, this one included, and the workspace service adds the project, so an answer and its event never both change the list. A connection that missed the event reloads the workspace at its next `workspace.subscribed`.

The tests ([`creation-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/creation-ipc.test.ts)) use a fake account service that builds each request with the real allowlist, and once the real workspace service. They cover the two channels and no other, the contract's limit against the protocol's, the sender check and every argument refusal before anything is sent, a name of 100 characters in code points, each create's exact route, path parameters, and body, the display name from the account status and nothing sent without a signed-in account, a project's name sent normalized, the protocol's check of the ID and of the name, `not_selected`, every failure of each create mapped to its fixed result and every code of each route to its own refusal, the add and the selection of a new workspace with the waiting messages kept, no selection while messages wait, after a switch meanwhile, or without a device in the workspace service, a project's ID only and a project of another workspace as `unknown`, an uncertain answer and a throw never sent again and reported with a fixed message, the waits passed through, and with the real workspace service a new workspace listed first, selected, subscribed, and loaded again, and a new project listed through its event without a read.

## Invitations

The invitations of #95 (ADR 0014, "Invitations"; decisions D1 to D3) are in the main process: the selected workspace's live invitations in the invitation service ([`src/main/invitations/service.ts`](src/main/invitations/service.ts)), which reach the renderer through `window.desktop.invitations` ([`src/main/invitation-ipc.ts`](src/main/invitation-ipc.ts)), and four writes of the [allowlist](#account), each behind its own strict channel, through `window.desktop.invitationActions` ([`src/main/invitation-actions-ipc.ts`](src/main/invitation-actions-ipc.ts); see [IPC contract](#ipc-contract)). The [people screen](#people-screen) shows the list and offers the create and the revocation, and the [join screen](#join-screen) uses the preview and the accept. [`index.ts`](src/main/index.ts) creates the service after the [workspace service](#workspaces) and closes it at quit (`will-quit`).

- **The code (D2).** An invitation code crosses IPC in two places only: from the main process to the renderer once, in the answer of a create, and from the renderer to the main process as the text that the person pasted, for a preview and an accept, where it goes on in the body of the write only. The API's list of live invitations holds no code. The main process never keeps, logs, or publishes one: no state holds it, an answer that throws is reported with a fixed message, and no refusal repeats an argument. The contract accepts only a text in the exact form of the protocol's code (`InvitationCodeTextSchema`, at most 48 characters, checked before the pattern runs); trimming a pasted text is the join screen's job.
- **Who sees the list.** Only the owner and the admins of a workspace may list its invitations. The service reads the role of the account's own person from the workspace service's people: while it is a member, or a former member, nothing loads and the state is `not_allowed`; while the people have not loaded, the state is `loading`. A demotion drops the list at once, and a promotion loads it. The service does not wait for the people to show its own demotion (#261): a `workspace.member.role_changed` to `member` of the account's own person drops the list as it arrives, also while the workspace service's [resync gate](#workspace-resync) holds that event during a reload, and the access stays `not_allowed` until the people show the role, the gate is live again, or an event promotes the person; another selected workspace and a sign-out end it. A load waits until the workspace service's gate is live (`gateLive`), so that it never reads with a role that the workspace's reload is about to change; after a reconnect that follows a demotion, nothing is read at all. So while the selected workspace's view reloads, or its reload keeps failing, a request answers `loading` and nothing is read: the list stays `loading`, or keeps what it held, until the view recovers.
- **When it loads (D3).** Nothing is read until the screen asks (`load`, when it opens and on the person's refresh). A request is refused as `not_signed_in`, `no_workspace`, or `not_allowed` without a read, but it counts, so the list loads once it may; within 2 seconds of the last request that was not refused it is `too_soon` (`INVITATION_LOAD_COOLDOWN_MS`). From then on, while the application runs, the list loads again after each `workspace.subscribed` of the selected workspace, on each `workspace.member.claimed` of it, because a claim consumes an invitation, and after a create or a revocation. Since the decision of 2026-11-10 it also loads again on each `workspace.member.removed` of the selected workspace, which a removal and a leave send, and on each `workspace.member.role_changed` of it to `member`, a demotion, because the server then revokes invitations (ADR 0014): a removal revokes the person's own invitation and those that the person created, and a demotion those that the admin created; a promotion to admin is no such trigger. An event about the account's own person reads nothing: its demotion drops the list as it arrives, and its removal ends the membership. A promotion to admin, and the role once the people loaded, load it as well.
- **Merged and backed off.** As the workspace service's `reload`: while a load is on its way, one more follows its answer, which is dropped, however many triggers came; while a retry waits out its backoff, the retry reads the change. A failed load keeps what was loaded, shows its failure as `unavailable` with the problem, and is retried with the workspace service's backoff (`workspaceRetryDelayMs`) while connected; offline, the next `workspace.subscribed` loads. After `rate_limited`, also when a trigger came during the load, the next load waits 60 seconds. A list that names another workspace is `failed`. Another selected workspace, a sign-out, or another device drops the list, the answers on their way, and a load that waits for the gate.
- **View.** `{ revision, status, problem?, workspaceId, invitations }`, with each invitation's ID, the member ID of the unclaimed person that it claims, its creator's display name, resolved with the workspace service's `memberName` (the creator's member ID stays in the main process), and its creation and expiry times. An invitation past its expiry is left out. A change of the service or of the workspace service, which reports a new name, publishes at most one view every 50 ms.
- **Create and revoke.** `create(workspaceId, memberId, lifetimeSeconds)` takes the unclaimed person, which is what the protocol's create takes, and a lifetime of 1 hour to 7 days, or null for the API's 7 days; the channel always takes all three, and the bridge's `lifetimeSeconds` may be left out, which sends null; `revoke(workspaceId, invitationId)` takes the invitation. The main process checks each ID again with the protocol's ID schemas (`invalid`) and refuses a workspace that is not the selected one as `not_selected`; the account service checks the lifetime with the protocol's request schema. A confirmed create answers the invitation's ID, its code, and its expiry only; an answer for another workspace or person is `unknown`, and its code is dropped. A new invitation for a person revokes the previous one, so a create is never sent again on its own: an uncertain answer is `unknown`. The refusals (`INVITATION_ACTION_REFUSALS`) are the account service's own and the API's `forbidden` as `not_allowed`, `not_found`, `conflict`, and `rate_limited`, which are the codes that these routes answer; any other refusal is `failed`. The list reloads after `ok`, `unknown`, `not_found`, and `conflict`.
- **Preview and accept.** They are not tied to the selected workspace, because the person is joining one. The main process checks the pasted text with the protocol's `InvitationCodeSchema` exactly (`invalid`). A preview answers the workspace's name, the display name of the person that the code claims, the inviter's display name and GitHub login with the time it was last verified, and the expiry; an accept answers the joined workspace's ID and name, never the person's user ID. The refusals (`JOIN_INVITATION_REFUSALS`) are the account service's own, `invitation_invalid`, `already_member`, and `rate_limited`, which also covers the API's limit of 10 failed previews or accepts in 15 minutes; any other refusal is `failed`. After an accept that answers `ok` or `unknown`, the workspace service loads the list again (`reloadList`), because no account frame reports the new membership.
- **Logs.** Fixed text with a failure code at most, never a name, an ID, or a code.

The tests ([`service.test.ts`](src/main/invitations/service.test.ts), [`invitation-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/invitation-ipc.test.ts), and [`invitation-actions-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/invitation-actions-ipc.test.ts)) use fake account services, one of which builds each write with the real allowlist, and once the real workspace and invitation services. They cover the sender check and every argument refusal before anything is sent, also of texts close to a code; the protocol's check of the IDs and the code again; `not_selected`; each action's exact route, path parameters, and body; every failure's result and each route's codes; the confirmed answers; one request after an uncertain answer and after one that throws, with a fixed report; the waits; the reloads after the actions and the list's reload after an accept; the code in exactly one create answer and nowhere in a published state, a log line, a reported error, or a refusal; the role filter; the own demotion's event in both orders with the workspace service, while its gate holds the event during a reload and after it applied it, a promotion that only the people show, one missed while offline that only a live gate shows, and one by an event after a demotion, the end of a demotion with another workspace and with a sign-out, and the demotion of another admin during a reload, which drops nothing; the loads that wait for the gate, also after a reconnect that follows a demotion, and their end with the workspace; the triggers, their merging, the backoff, and the drops; the creators' names, the expiry, and the coalesced publish; and that the contract's limits and code form match the protocol.

## Join requests

The join requests of #95 (ADR 0014, "Join requests"; decisions D1 and D3, and the Project Manager's decisions of 2026-11-09 on #210 and #227) have their side of the owner and the admins in the main process: the selected workspace's pending requests and blocks in force in the join request service ([`src/main/join-requests/service.ts`](src/main/join-requests/service.ts)), which reach the renderer through `window.desktop.joinRequests` ([`src/main/join-request-ipc.ts`](src/main/join-request-ipc.ts)), and seven writes of the [allowlist](#account), each behind its own strict channel, through `window.desktop.joinRequestActions` ([`src/main/join-request-actions-ipc.ts`](src/main/join-request-actions-ipc.ts); see [IPC contract](#ipc-contract)). No screen uses them yet: a later pull request of #95 adds the join requests to the [people screen](#people-screen). The requester's side, a request by workspace ID and the account's own requests with their withdrawal, follows in its own pull request. [`index.ts`](src/main/index.ts) creates the service after the [invitation service](#invitations) and closes it at quit (`will-quit`).

- **Who sees the list.** Only the owner and the admins may list a workspace's join requests and decide on them. The service reads the role of the account's own person from the workspace service's people, as the invitation service does: while it is a member, or a former member, nothing loads and the state is `not_allowed`; while the people have not loaded, the state is `loading`. A demotion drops the list and the remembered IDs at once, and a promotion loads it. While the role is `member` or not known, the join request events and the triggers are ignored (the decision of 2026-11-09 on #210), because a person whose demotion committed during a change can still get that change's event. The service does not wait for the people to show its own demotion: a `workspace.member.role_changed` to `member` of the account's own person drops the list as it arrives, also while the workspace service's [resync gate](#workspace-resync) holds that event during a reload, and the access stays `not_allowed` until the people show the role, the gate is live again, or an event promotes the person. A load waits until the workspace service's gate is live (`gateLive`), so that it never reads with a role that the workspace's reload is about to change; after a reconnect that follows a demotion, nothing is read at all.
- **When it loads (D3).** Nothing is read, and no event applies, until the screen asks (`load`, when it opens and on the person's refresh). A request is refused as `not_signed_in`, `no_workspace`, or `not_allowed` without a read, but it counts, so the list loads once it may; within 2 seconds of the last request that was not refused it is `too_soon` (`JOIN_REQUEST_LOAD_COOLDOWN_MS`). From then on, while the application runs, the list loads again after each `workspace.subscribed` of the selected workspace, on each `workspace.member.claimed` of it, because a requester who joins with an invitation leaves the list without an event, on each `workspace.member.removed` of another person in it (the decision of 2026-11-09), and after a decision (approve, deny, deny all, block, unblock) that answers `ok`, `unknown`, `not_found`, `conflict`, or `already_member`. `not_found` and `conflict` cover a request that ended without an event, such as one that the erasure of an account withdrew, and an unblock of a block that another approver lifted. The removal of the account's own person reads nothing, because it ends the membership; a role change of another person is no trigger.
- **Events (#210, #227).** `workspace.join_request.created` adds the request at the end of the pending requests, unless it is held or past its expiry; when 1,000 requests are held (`MAX_HELD_JOIN_REQUESTS`), it loads the list again instead, so that the server decides which requests one answer holds. `workspace.join_request.resolved` ends a pending request; with `blocked`, the request becomes a block in force, blocked at the event's `occurredAt`, which is the time that the server stores as its `decidedAt`. `workspace.join_request.unblocked` ends a block. Each sets the state that it reports, so a repeated event changes nothing, and an event for a request that is not held changes nothing either. Events of another workspace change nothing.
- **Late events.** The ID of a resolved request and of an unblocked request is remembered as it arrives, also before the first load answered and when that load fails, for 5 minutes (`JOIN_REQUEST_MEMORY_MS`, at most 1,000 of each, `JOIN_REQUEST_MEMORY_LIMIT`, the oldest going first), so that a `created` that arrives after its `resolved`, or a `resolved` with `blocked` after its `unblocked`, brings nothing back; a load's answer is filtered the same way. A request ID never becomes pending again, and a lifted block never comes back, so the memory only bounds the memory used. The events that arrive while a load is on its way apply at once to the list that shows, and again on top of the load's answer, so that an answer read before them does not undo them (the protocol's resynchronization rule); beyond 1,000 such events (`MAX_REPLAYED_JOIN_REQUEST_EVENTS`), the answer is dropped and one more load follows.
- **Expiry.** Expiry has no event (#210): a pending request leaves the list at its `expiresAt` by the system clock, through one timer for the soonest expiry, which waits at most a day (`JOIN_REQUEST_EXPIRY_CHECK_MAX_MS`) and then checks again. A `created` past its expiry adds nothing. A load's answer is not filtered by the system clock, because the server left out the requests past their expiry by its own; the timer then follows the system clock, so a clock that runs ahead still drops a request early. A block has no expiry.
- **Merged and backed off.** As the invitation service: while a load is on its way, one more follows its answer, which is dropped, however many triggers came; while a retry waits out its backoff, the retry reads the change. A failed load keeps what was loaded, shows its failure as `unavailable` with the problem, and is retried with the workspace service's backoff (`workspaceRetryDelayMs`) while connected; offline, the next `workspace.subscribed` loads. After `rate_limited`, also when a trigger came during the load, the next load waits 60 seconds. A list that names another workspace is `failed`. Another selected workspace, a sign-out, or another device drops the list, the remembered IDs, and the answers on their way.
- **The pause.** `pausedAt` is the time when new requests were paused, from the [workspace service](#workspaces)'s `joinRequestsPausedAt`, which it keeps from each reload of the view, and null while they are accepted. The service passes it on only for the owner and the admins. No event reports a pause or its end, so a pause and a resume reload the view (`reload`), and the other devices of the owner and the admins see it at their next reload. The Project Manager decided on 2026-11-12 that this stays so for now: a stale pause line is harmless, because both actions can be repeated safely and the view reloads after them. An event added later would go to every member, because members also read `joinRequestsPausedAt` from the view.
- **View.** `{ revision, status, problem?, workspaceId, pending, blocked, pausedAt }`. Each pending request crosses as `{ joinRequestId, requestedName, login, verifiedAt, accountCreatedAt, createdAt, expiresAt }`: the requested display name, the requester's GitHub login with the time it was last verified, and the creation date of that GitHub account, which ADR 0014 shows next to the requested name. Each block crosses as `{ joinRequestId, requestedName, login, verifiedAt, accountCreatedAt, createdAt, blockedAt }`, where the login and its times are null only after the account's data was erased. No user ID crosses. A pending request past its expiry is left out when the view is built as well. Each list holds at most 1,000 (`JOIN_REQUEST_LIMITS`), and a name is cut to 64 characters. A change of the service publishes at most one view every 50 ms.
- **Actions (D1).** `approve(workspaceId, joinRequestId, displayName)` takes a display name of 1 to 64 characters that replaces the requested one, or null for the requested one; `deny`, `block`, and `unblock` take the workspace ID and the request ID; `denyAll`, `pause`, and `resume` the workspace ID. The main process checks each ID again with the protocol's `WorkspaceIdSchema` and `JoinRequestIdSchema` (`invalid`) and refuses a workspace that is not the selected one as `not_selected`; the account service checks the name with the protocol's request schema, which also refuses a blank name and control, format, and invisible characters (`invalid`). Each action is one `sendWorkspaceAction`, sent once: an uncertain answer, and one that throws, is `unknown`, and nothing sends it again. A confirmed approval answers the new person's member ID only, never its user ID; an answer that names another workspace is `unknown`. The refusals (`JOIN_REQUEST_ACTION_REFUSALS`) are the account service's own, and the API's `forbidden` as `not_allowed`, `not_found`, `conflict`, `already_member` (an approval of an account that is already an active member), and `rate_limited`, which are the codes that these routes answer; any other refusal is `failed`. Nothing changes the held requests on an answer: the events do, and the reloads above.
- **Logs.** Fixed text with a failure code at most, never a name, a login, or an ID.

"Deny and invite instead" is not a channel of its own: the API has no route for it (ADR 0014 and the decision of #92), and the channels it needs exist. The Project Manager decided on 2026-11-12 that the screen composes it, the invitation first: it defines the person with `peopleActions.define` when needed, then sends `invitationActions.create`, whose answer carries the code once (D2), and only then `joinRequestActions.deny`. When a step fails, the harmless state "invited, the request still pending" remains. The reverse order risks "denied, and no invitation", and a denied account can ask the workspace again only after 24 hours.

The tests ([`service.test.ts`](src/main/join-requests/service.test.ts), [`join-request-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/join-request-ipc.test.ts), and [`join-request-actions-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/join-request-actions-ipc.test.ts)) use a fake workspace service, connection, and account reads with the events built through `decodeServerMessage`, and a fake account service that builds each write with the real allowlist. They cover nothing read before the screen asks, the role filter for the events, the triggers, and the reads (a member, a role not known yet, a demotion, and a removed own person), the own demotion's event in both orders with the workspace service, while its gate holds the event and after it applied it, a promotion that only the people show and one by an event after a demotion, the loads that wait for the gate, also after a reconnect that follows a demotion, the refusals and the cooldown, each event's effect, a repeated event, an event for a request that is not held, and events of another workspace, the remembered IDs against a late `created`, a late `resolved` with `blocked`, and a load's answer, also after a first load that failed, their end, and their limit of 1,000 with the oldest forgotten, a `created` that does not fit beside 1,000 held requests, live and on a load's answer, the events during a load applied again on its answer, also during the first load, and the overflow, each trigger and those that are none, the merging and the backoff, `rate_limited`, offline, the expiry by the clock, a created event past it, and an answer that is not filtered by the clock, the drops, the pause for the owner and the admins only, a block of an erased account, a decision that the list does not show, `close`, and logs without a name or an ID; for the view, every field, the expiry, the cut names, the limits, the statuses and problems, that no user ID crosses, and the coalesced publish; and for the actions, the sender check and every argument refusal before anything is sent, the protocol's check of the IDs again, a blank name or one with a line feed or an invisible character answered `invalid`, `not_selected`, each action's exact route, path parameters, and body, every failure's result and each route's codes, the approval's member ID without a user ID, one request after an uncertain answer and after one that throws, with a fixed report, and the reloads after each result.

## Own join requests

The requester's side of the join requests of #95 (ADR 0014, "Join requests" and "What a workspace ID allows"; decisions D1 and D3, and the Project Manager's decisions of 2026-11-01 on #92) is in the main process: the account's own join requests in the own join request service ([`src/main/own-join-requests/service.ts`](src/main/own-join-requests/service.ts)), which reach the renderer through `window.desktop.ownJoinRequests` ([`src/main/own-join-request-ipc.ts`](src/main/own-join-request-ipc.ts)), and two writes of the [allowlist](#account), each behind its own strict channel, through `window.desktop.ownJoinRequestActions` ([`src/main/own-join-request-actions-ipc.ts`](src/main/own-join-request-actions-ipc.ts); see [IPC contract](#ipc-contract)): asking to join a workspace by its ID, and withdrawing a pending request. No screen uses them yet; a later pull request adds them to the [join screen](#join-screen). [`index.ts`](src/main/index.ts) creates the service and closes it at quit (`will-quit`).

- **What is held.** The requests of `GET /join-requests` (`listOwnJoinRequests`), in the API's order, the most recently created first: each with its ID, the ID of the workspace that it asks to join, the requested display name, its creation and expiry times, and how it ended, `approved`, `denied`, `withdrawn`, or `expired`, with the time, or null twice while it is pending. A block shows as `denied`, as the API lists it (#92). The API shows the requester nothing else about the workspace before an approval, so no workspace name is held; after an approval the workspace appears in the [workspace list](#workspaces) with its name. The answer holds no user ID.
- **Expiry.** A request past its expiry stays in the list as `expired`, decided at its expiry, as the API lists it (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-12): a pending request becomes `expired` at its `expiresAt` by the system clock, through one timer for the soonest expiry, which waits at most a day (`OWN_JOIN_REQUEST_EXPIRY_CHECK_MAX_MS`) and then checks again, also before the API marks it, and so does a pending request of a load's answer that is already past it. An ended request never changes. The approvers' list of the join requests drops an expired request instead (#210).
- **When it loads (D3).** Nothing is read until the screen asks (`load`, when it opens and on the person's refresh). Without a signed-in device a request is refused as `not_signed_in`, but it counts, so the requests load once a device is; within 2 seconds of the last request that was not refused it is `too_soon` (`OWN_JOIN_REQUEST_LOAD_COOLDOWN_MS`). From then on, while the application runs, they load again when a device becomes signed in, when the connection reaches `connected`, because account frames sent while it was offline are lost, on each `account.join_request_decided`, and after a create that answers `ok` or `unknown` or a withdrawal that answers `ok`, `unknown`, `not_found`, or `conflict`. The frame is not applied to the held requests: the load reads the decision, so that an older answer cannot undo it, as the workspace service does after an ownership frame. The account's own `workspace.member.claimed` is no trigger: a device receives it only for a workspace that it is subscribed to, where the account is already an active member, and no claim ends a request.
- **Merged and backed off.** As the [device list service](#device-list-service), which also follows the account rather than a workspace: one load at a time, and none while a sign-in runs; a trigger during a load drops its answer and loads again; a failed load keeps what was loaded, shows its failure as `unavailable` with the problem, and is retried with the workspace service's backoff (`workspaceRetryDelayMs`) while connected, and offline the next `connected` loads. After `rate_limited`, also in the answer of a load that a trigger overtook, nothing loads until its 60-second wait has ended, and a trigger meanwhile loads at its end. A sign-out, another account, or another device drops the requests and the answers on their way.
- **View.** `{ revision, status, problem?, requests }`, with each request as `{ joinRequestId, workspaceId, requestedName, createdAt, expiresAt, decision, decidedAt }`. A pending request past its expiry crosses as `expired` when the view is built as well. The list holds at most 1,000 (`OWN_JOIN_REQUEST_LIMITS`), and a name is cut to 64 characters. A change of the service publishes at most one view every 50 ms.
- **Create (D1).** `create(workspaceId, displayName)` takes the ID of the workspace, as the person typed it, and the requested display name, or null for the account's display name: `users.display_name`, which the device record holds and the account status shows. A signed-in account always has that name (the device record holds it by the protocol's rule), so a create without a name while no account is signed in answers `not_signed_in`, and nothing is sent. The main process checks the ID again with the protocol's `WorkspaceIdSchema` (`invalid`); the account service checks the name with the protocol's request schema, counted in characters (code points), which also refuses a blank name and control, format, and invisible characters (`invalid`). A create is not tied to the selected workspace, because the account is not a member of the workspace that it names. While the account has a pending request for the workspace, the API answers with that request instead of creating another, so a confirmed create answers the pending request, `{ joinRequestId, workspaceId, requestedName, createdAt, expiresAt }`, whose name may differ from the one sent; an answer that names another workspace is `unknown`.
- **Withdraw (D1).** `withdraw(joinRequestId)` takes the ID of one of the account's own pending requests, which the main process checks again with the protocol's `JoinRequestIdSchema` (`invalid`).
- **Results.** Each action is one `sendWorkspaceAction`, sent once: an uncertain answer, and one that throws, is `unknown`, and nothing sends it again. The refusals (`OWN_JOIN_REQUEST_ACTION_REFUSALS`) are the account service's own (`invalid`, `not_signed_in`, `unavailable`, and `too_soon` with its wait), and the API's codes as they are: for a create `already_member`, `workspace_not_found` (an unknown or archived workspace), and `join_requests_closed` (a pause or a block, which the API does not tell apart); for a withdrawal `not_found` and `conflict` (a request that is no longer pending, also one past its expiry); for both `rate_limited` with its wait, which also covers the limits of ADR 0014: 5 pending requests and 10 new requests in 24 hours per account, 50 pending requests per workspace, and 24 hours after a denial. Any other refusal is `failed`, also `device_too_new`, which the protocol does not list for a create (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-12).
- **Logs.** Fixed text with a failure code at most, never a name or an ID.

The tests ([`service.test.ts`](src/main/own-join-requests/service.test.ts), [`own-join-request-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/own-join-request-ipc.test.ts), and [`own-join-request-actions-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/own-join-request-actions-ipc.test.ts)) use a fake account service and connection with the frames and events built through `decodeServerMessage`, an injected monotonic clock, system clock, and timer, and a fake account service that builds each write with the real allowlist. They cover nothing read before the screen asks, the refusals and the cooldown, the triggers and those that are none (the account's own claim, other frames, and a connection status other than a change to `connected`), the frame not applied, the merging, a running sign-in, the backoff, offline, `rate_limited` and a trigger during its wait, a read that rejects, the drops, the expiry by the clock, also of a request that an answer holds past it, the timer's longest wait, and an ended request that never changes, `close`, and logs without a name or an ID; for the view, every field, the expiry, the cut names, the limit, the statuses and problems, the outcomes against the protocol, that no user ID crosses, and the coalesced publish; and for the actions, the sender check and every argument refusal before anything is sent, the protocol's check of the IDs again, the account's display name as the default and `not_signed_in` without a signed-in account, `device_too_new` as `failed`, a blank name or one with a line feed or an invisible character answered `invalid`, each action's exact route, path parameters, and body, every failure's result and each route's codes, the confirmed create without a user ID and with its name cut, an answer for another workspace, one request after an uncertain answer and after one that throws, with a fixed report, and the reloads after each result.

## Agent activity service

The agent activity service ([`src/main/agents/service.ts`](src/main/agents/service.ts); #28) holds the agents and runs of the [workspace service](#workspaces)'s open project in the main process, in the model of [agent activity](#agent-activity). It reads them with the account service's [workspace reads](#account) and keeps them current with the run events of the selected workspace. It never sends an event. [`index.ts`](src/main/index.ts) creates it right after the workspace service and closes it at quit (`will-quit`). [`src/main/agent-ipc.ts`](src/main/agent-ipc.ts) sends its state to the renderer read-only as `window.desktop.agents`, with each owner's name resolved in the main process, the newest runs only, and at most one publish every 50 ms (see [IPC contract](#ipc-contract)). `gateLive()` is true only while the open project's activity was reloaded since the selected workspace's last `workspace.subscribed`, with no reload open; the development switch sends only then.

- **Gate.** The service owns its own [resync gate](#workspace-resync). After each `workspace.subscribed` of the selected workspace, it reloads the open project, if any. Opening another project begins a reload of everything. Closing the project, or another workspace, clears the state; another workspace also ends the gate until its own `workspace.subscribed`, and so do `workspace.unsubscribed`, `workspace.subscribe_refused`, and a lost connection. A project opened before the subscription loads at once, and its events apply only after the next `workspace.subscribed` and its reload.
- **Reload.** `GET /projects/:projectId/runs` first, then `GET /projects/:projectId/agents`, so that the agent list is never older than the runs. The answers go through the model, and the events that waited apply after them, in order. One reload runs at a time: a reload that begins while another is on its way waits for it, and a stale reload stops after its runs. An answer for an earlier generation, another project, another workspace, or another device is dropped; answers that hold another project's agents or runs are refused as `failed`.
- **Events.** `agent.run.state_changed` of the selected workspace goes to the model. `unknown_agent` is ignored: most often the run belongs to another project, because the event names none. `unknown_run` reloads everything, once for each reload: an `unknown_run` after the reload that an `unknown_run` began reloads nothing more, so data that stays inconsistent, such as a run beyond the list's limit, does not reload in a loop. The model's `agentsStale`, `workspace.member.removed`, and `account.device_revoked` reload the agent list alone (a part reload); the gate may widen it to everything. A revocation disables the device's agents but cancels only their unfinished runs, so without one, the account's own frame is what reports it. Both reloads wait one second (`AGENT_FOLLOW_UP_DELAY_MS`), so that a burst of events, such as the cancellations of a removed person's runs, reloads once, and a full reload that begins meanwhile replaces a waiting part reload.
- **Backoff.** A failed reload is retried with the workspace service's backoff (`workspaceRetryDelayMs`): min(60 s, 2 s x 2^n), and 60 seconds after `rate_limited`. The retry reloads everything. An overflow of the gate's buffer reloads after the same backoff.
