- **Reset.** A sign-out or another device clears everything and drops the answers on their way, and the next reload does not wait for them.
- **State.** `{ revision, status, problem?, workspaceId, projectId, agents, unfinishedRuns, finishedRuns }`, frozen, with `onChange` listeners. `status` is `no_project`, `loading` until the open project's activity loaded once, `ready`, or `unavailable` with the failure as `problem` until a reload succeeds (a retry waits, or the next `workspace.subscribed` reloads); what was loaded before stays. An agent's owner is a member ID: the IPC resolves names with the workspace service's `memberName`, so the state holds no person's name.
- **Logs.** Fixed text with a failure code at most, never a name or an ID.

Not in this part: the agent panel ([Screens](#screens)), the [development agent switch](#development-agent-switch) and every send, and agent events beyond the run state, which the protocol does not have yet. That is also a gap: disabling an agent has no event, so when another person's device with no unfinished run is revoked, the agent list stays stale until the next reload.

The tests ([`service.test.ts`](src/main/agents/service.test.ts)) use a fake account service with reads that the test answers, a fake connection client, a fake workspace service, and injected timers, and build every answer through the protocol's schemas and every frame and event through `decodeServerMessage`. They cover the order runs then agents, the reload after every `workspace.subscribed` and without one before a project opens, events that wait for a reload, one reload at a time with the dropped answer of an earlier generation, of the previous project, and of the previous workspace, `unknown_agent` ignored, `unknown_run` with one reload for each reload, the part reload after `agentsStale` (also for another project's run), after `workspace.member.removed` (also when it waited for a reload), and after `account.device_revoked`, a full follow-up that stays full when a part reload is asked for within the delay, an `unknown_run` that waited for the reload that an `unknown_run` began, the backoff (started again by another project) with 60 seconds after `rate_limited`, a refused answer, and a read that throws, the overflow, the lost connection, the ended and the refused subscription (and those of another workspace, which change nothing), `workspace.subscribed` during a retry, another workspace with no open project, the sign-out, a key that is not ready, a missing API origin, and another device (whose reload still runs alone), `gateLive()` (before the subscription, during a reload of everything and of the agents alone, after a lost connection, a closed project, and `close`), a listener that throws and one that changes the state again, `close`, and that no log holds a name or an ID, the state holds no person's name, and the file neither logs with `console` nor sends.

## Development agent switch

The development agent switch ([`src/main/agents/dev-switch.ts`](src/main/agents/dev-switch.ts); #28, decisions D2, D4, and D5 of 2026-11-02 and the Project Manager's decisions of 2026-11-05) is the test source of the [agent activity](#agent-activity) until #35 lets agents run. In an unpackaged development build, it moves the unfinished runs of the agent that `db:dev-seed` created through allowed transitions, as that agent, from its host device, over the real [connection](#connection). No production route is added.

**Use.**

1. Start the API with a local database and sign in on the desktop. Create a workspace and a project with `db:dev-workspace`, and the agent with its runs with `db:dev-seed`, both with this device's ID ([database README](../../packages/database/README.md#development-seed); "Find the device ID" tells where the desktop keeps it).
2. Start the desktop with the agent ID that `db:dev-seed` printed and the device ID that it was given:

   ```sh
   MYMANDEV_DEV_AGENT_SWITCH=<agent ID> MYMANDEV_DEV_AGENT_DEVICE=<device ID> npm run dev -w @mymandev/desktop
   ```

   In PowerShell, set `$env:MYMANDEV_DEV_AGENT_SWITCH` and `$env:MYMANDEV_DEV_AGENT_DEVICE` first. `MYMANDEV_API_ORIGIN` is set as for any local API.

3. Open the seeded project. The switch walks the agent's oldest unfinished run through `queued`, `running`, `awaiting_input`, `running`, and `completed`, one transition every 2 seconds, and then the next run. Its log lines in the terminal say what it does. `db:dev-seed` creates a new agent each time, so start the desktop again with the new agent ID.

**Guards.** Each one holds before every send, resends included.

- **Development build.** [`index.ts`](src/main/index.ts) creates the switch only when `app.isPackaged` is false, and `devAgentSwitchSettings` checks it again before it reads the environment. The switch has no IPC channel: the renderer cannot start it, stop it, or see it, and a packaged build sends nothing.
- **Variables.** `MYMANDEV_DEV_AGENT_SWITCH` must hold an agent ID and `MYMANDEV_DEV_AGENT_DEVICE` a device ID, each checked with the protocol's schema (`AgentIdSchema`, `DeviceIdSchema`: a lowercase UUIDv7). Without the first one, or with it empty, the switch stays off without a word. When the first one is set, a value that is not an ID of its kind logs a fixed line, and the switch stays off; so does a `MYMANDEV_DEV_AGENT_DEVICE` that is unset or empty.
- **Host device.** The server closes the connection with `identity_mismatch` and audits it when an agent actor names an agent that another device hosts. The agent list shows the host device to no account, the owner included (#110), and no other route shows it, so `MYMANDEV_DEV_AGENT_DEVICE` names it: the device ID that `db:dev-seed` was given. The switch sends only while the signed-in device is that device, and the `build` that it passes to `sendAs` checks the socket's identity again and builds nothing for another device. The second variable is an addition to the decision of 2026-11-05, which names the agent ID only, because the desktop has no other way to know the host device.
- **Owner, and not disabled.** The open project's agent list, from the [agent activity service](#agent-activity-service), must hold the agent with `disabledAt` null and with `ownerMemberId` equal to the account's own person in the workspace, which the [workspace service](#workspaces)'s `ownMemberId` gives from `GET /workspaces`.
- **Live.** The workspace service's `gateLive(workspaceId)` and the agent activity service's `gateLive()` must both hold. Otherwise the switch waits: closing the project pauses it, and opening it again resumes it. A sign-out and a reload pause it the same way. A closed or lost connection stops it for good instead (see **Sends**).

**Sends.**

- Every send goes through the connection client's `sendAs`, with the agent actor (`{ kind: "agent", agentId }`) and the identity's device ID. A new transition gets a new UUIDv7 event ID and the wall clock as `occurredAt` when it is sent; it carries no reason, repository, correlation ID, or causation ID.
- One transition at a time: the next one waits for the acknowledgement of the last one, the relayed `agent.run.state_changed` with the same event ID, the agent actor, the same run, and the same workspace, which the sending connection gets also when it is not subscribed (D4). The acknowledgement's payload is the run's state from then on, also before the agent activity applies the relayed event, for example while its reload holds events back; a run without one has the agent activity's state. Nothing is taken from what was sent.
- Without an acknowledgement within 10 seconds (`DEV_SWITCH_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS`) while the connection stays open, it sends the same event again, with the same event ID and time, once the guards hold; the server never applies a recorded (device, event ID) twice and relays it again (D4). Without an acknowledgement of that resend within another 10 seconds, it stops.
- It stops at the first refusal or close, as decided on 2026-11-05. Any close or loss of the open connection stops it for good, whether a transition waits for its acknowledgement or not, and also while it is paused: every close that the client reports as `waiting` (for example `connection_lost` after 1006 or 1001, `server_error` after 1011, `server_busy` after 1013, and `message_refused` after 1008 such as `identity_mismatch`, when the database names another host device than `MYMANDEV_DEV_AGENT_DEVICE`) or as `stopped` (for example `connection_replaced` or `device_revoked`), and the client's own close and reopen, which it reports as `connecting` (for example after the system resumed). Nothing is sent again after a close, because the server may have applied a transition that waited, and the reconnect does not resume the switch. Only the client's close for the account, `offline` after a sign-out, a device removal, "This is not me", or a key that is not ready, pauses it like the account guard, unless a transition waits for its acknowledgement: then it stops as well. A connection that was not open yet, such as a first attempt that fails, changes nothing.
- It stops at the first `event.refused` of its event (D5): `conflict` for a stale `previousState` or a disabled agent, `not_found` for an unknown run, and `rate_limited`. The further decisions of 2026-11-02 added the disabled agent and `rate_limited`. It also stops when the client does not send (`not_connected`, `backpressure`, `too_large`, or `invalid`), and on close at quit (`will-quit`). It stays stopped until the application starts again.
- At least 2 seconds pass between two sends, resends included (`DEV_SWITCH_STEP_MS`), so at most 5 go out in any 10 seconds, against the account's 30. At most 900 go out in any hour (`DEV_SWITCH_HOURLY_SENDS`), half of the account's 1,800, so that the account's other devices keep room. The account's two limits for transitions are the values that the Project Manager approved on 2026-11-05 (#165, #168).
- **Logs.** Fixed text with a refusal code, a send outcome, or a connection code at most: what the switch waits for, with its own line for a device that is not signed in and ready, another device, an open project that does not list the agent, an owner that is not the person, a disabled agent, and a project that is not open or not loaded yet, each logged once per change of reason; that it moves the runs; that no unfinished run is left; a resend; and why it stopped. Never an ID or a name.

**Never.** The switch never creates or cancels a run, sends for another agent or from another device, sends in a packaged build or while a gate is not live, sends a second transition before the first one's acknowledgement, sends anything after a refusal or a close, keeps anything across restarts, or adds a route to the API.

The tests ([`dev-switch.test.ts`](src/main/agents/dev-switch.test.ts)) use a fake account service, a fake connection client whose `sendAs` builds from an identity and checks each event with `decodeClientMessage`, fake workspace and agent activity services, and injected timers and clocks, and build every acknowledgement and refusal through `decodeServerMessage`. They cover the settings in a packaged build and with an unset, empty, or invalid variable, another device (also only in the socket's identity), a sign-out, another owner, no own person, a disabled agent, both gates, the walk of two runs and of runs that are already further on, the 2-second pace, the wait for each acknowledgement and relays that are none (another event, agent, run, workspace, or event type), the one resend with the same event and the stop after it, the stop for good at every `waiting` and every `stopped` reason and at a reopen of the open connection, with and without a transition that waits, while paused, and while a resend waits for a gate, with no resend and no resume after the reconnect, the pause at a sign-out and the stop at a sign-out while a transition waits, a connection that was not open yet, a resend that waits for the hourly budget, the account status checks during a walk (a key that is not ready, no API origin, a device that is not signed in), each reason to wait with its own line, once per change, a log that throws, the stop at each refusal and each refused send, the pause and the resume with the project, an acknowledgement while paused, the run states from the acknowledgements' payloads, the budget over more than an hour of sends against both limits, `close`, that no log holds an ID or a name and the file does not log with `console`, and that only `index.ts` imports the switch. The tests of the [workspace service](#workspaces) and of the [agent activity service](#agent-activity-service) also cover `ownMemberId` and `gateLive()`.

## Chat service

The chat service ([`src/main/chat/service.ts`](src/main/chat/service.ts); #27) holds the chat of the open project in the main process: the project's [chat timeline](#chat-timeline), its history read with the account service's [workspace reads](#account), its `chat.message.created` events, and the person's messages, sent with the connection client's `sendAs`, the only way to send an event. It follows the [workspace service](#workspaces): the selected workspace and its open project. [`index.ts`](src/main/index.ts) creates it after the workspace service and closes it at quit (`will-quit`). [`src/main/chat-ipc.ts`](src/main/chat-ipc.ts) sends its state to the renderer as `window.desktop.chat`, with each author's name resolved in the main process, within a text budget, and at most one publish every 50 ms, and takes the renderer's sends, retries, discards, and requests for older messages (see [IPC contract](#ipc-contract)).

- **Key.** A chat belongs to the account, the device, the workspace, and the project. The account and the device come from the connection client's `identity()` after `auth.ok` for the signed-in device, and are kept while the connection is down, so that messages can wait under their key. Without a signed-in device or such an identity the service holds nothing.
- **History.** The newest page (`GET /projects/:projectId/messages`, 50 messages) loads when a project opens and after every `workspace.subscribed` of the selected workspace, through the service's own [resync gate](#workspace-resync): `begin("all")` on a change of the open project, `subscribed()` on `workspace.subscribed`, and `end()` on a lost connection, an unsubscription, or another workspace. The project's `chat.message.created` events that arrive meanwhile wait and apply after the page; other projects' events are not held. An answer of an earlier generation, or for another project, is dropped. A failed page is retried with the workspace service's backoff (`workspaceRetryDelayMs`: min(60 s, 2 s x 2^n), and 60 seconds after `rate_limited`), and an overflow of the gate reloads after the same backoff. `loadOlder()` reads the page before the lowest held `seq`; its answer is dropped when the gate's `generation()` or the project changed meanwhile.
- **Sending.** `send(text)` makes the body with `prepareMessageBody`, so the limits are the protocol's, and a new UUIDv7 event ID (`newId`). A body that the protocol refuses, and a message beyond 50 that have not been stored (`too_many_pending`), are refused and nothing is kept. A message goes out at once when no other waits before it and the workspace service's `gateLive` holds for its workspace; otherwise it waits in line. Each event is built inside `sendAs` from the connection's identity, and only when that identity is the chat's own; another one drops the chat's waiting messages instead.
- **Waiting messages** (the Project Manager's decisions of 2026-11-05 on #27). They wait in memory, marked as waiting for the connection, and are sent again automatically with the same event ID and the same `occurredAt` while `gateLive` holds, at most one per second (`CHAT_RESEND_INTERVAL_MS`). The waiting messages go out in the order the person wrote them: only the first waiting message is sent, and while it waits for its retry after `rate_limited`, the messages behind it, a new one included, wait for it. Two limits remain. A message that shows as not sent no longer holds the line, so after a later `retry` the server stores it after the messages that were behind it. A message that is being sent does not hold the line either: in a narrow case the server's limit is reached within one round trip, the first message is then refused with `rate_limited`, and the next one is stored first. Waiting for each message's answer before the next is sent would cost a round trip per message and need its own way out for a lost answer, so the service does not. A message that was sent waits again when the connection drops. After an overflow of the gate, a message that was being sent at the overflow, or that was sent while the gate stayed overflowed, waits again once the reload has answered, because its `chat.message.created` may have been dropped; one sent after the reload began is not sent twice. The server answers a repeated event ID with the stored message. The waiting messages and their key outlive a lost connection, also when the workspace service changes its state while offline. `chat.message.created` removes the copy whose event ID is its `causationId`.
- **Refusals.** `not_found` and `conflict` show the message as not sent at once. After `rate_limited` it is tried again 10 seconds later (`CHAT_RATE_LIMITED_RETRY_MS`), up to 3 times (`CHAT_RATE_LIMITED_RETRIES`), so it is sent 4 times in all; after the third retry's refusal it shows as not sent. A late `rate_limited` for a message that is not being sent changes nothing: one that shows as not sent stays so, and a second answer for a message that an overflow had sent twice does not count against its retries. Discarding the first waiting message lets the next one go at once. A message that the connection client refuses as `too_large` or `invalid` shows as not sent as `not_sendable`. `retry(eventId)` sends a message that shows as not sent again, with the same event ID and new retries, and `discard(eventId)` drops a message that waits or shows as not sent.
- **Drops.** The waiting messages are dropped without asking on a sign-out, on a change of the account or the device, when the membership ends (`workspace.unsubscribed` with `membership_ended`, `account.removed_from_workspace`, or `workspace.subscribe_refused`), and whenever the workspace service selects another workspace or closes the project by itself. When the window closes while messages wait, a native dialog asks first, and the page's reload, a failed load, and a gone render process drop them without a question ([IPC contract](#ipc-contract)). They are lost when the application quits; nothing is written to disk.
- **Switching.** When messages have not been stored yet and the person opens another project or selects another workspace, the application asks first. `switchProject(projectId | null, { discardWaiting })` and `selectWorkspace(workspaceId, { discardWaiting })` answer `{ kind: "waiting", count }` and change nothing while messages wait and `discardWaiting` is false; otherwise they drop the messages and call the workspace service, and answer `switched`, or `refused` for a project or a workspace that it does not hold. The IPC and the screens must switch through these wrappers, never through the workspace service's `openProject` and `select`: the workspace IPC's `select` and `openProject` do, and answer `waiting` with the count (see [IPC contract](#ipc-contract)).
- **State.** `{ revision, status, problem?, workspaceId, projectId, messages, hasOlder, loadingOlder, olderProblem?, pending }`, frozen, with `onChange` listeners. `status` is `no_project`, `loading` until the newest page loaded, `ready`, or `unavailable` with the failure as `problem`; what was loaded before stays. `messages` are the timeline's, the oldest first, so the IPC can drop the oldest ones to fit its byte budget. `pending` holds `{ eventId, body, status }` with `waiting`, `sending`, or `not_sent` with a `reason`. `authorName(author)` gives a person's name from the workspace service (`Unknown person` when it does not hold the person) and `Unknown agent` (`UNKNOWN_AGENT_NAME`) for an agent.
- **Logs.** Fixed text with a failure code at most, never a name, an ID, or a message body.

The [chat screen](#chat-screen) shows the chat and asks the question before a switch. Not in this part: agent names (#28) and unread markers (none in M2).

The tests ([`service.test.ts`](src/main/chat/service.test.ts)) use a fake account service with reads that the test answers, a fake connection client whose `sendAs` builds each event from the socket's identity and checks it with `decodeClientMessage`, a fake workspace service with `gateLive`, and injected timers and clocks, and build every page through the protocol's schema and every frame and event through `decodeServerMessage`. They cover the load when a project opens and not without a signed-in device and its identity, the reload after every `workspace.subscribed` with the events that wait for it, the dropped answers of an earlier generation and of another project, the backoff with 60 seconds after `rate_limited`, older pages and their late answers, a send with the connection's identity and its replacement through `causationId`, the body rules, the limit of 50, sends only while `gateLive` holds with the same event IDs at one per second, a new message behind waiting ones and behind a message that waits after `rate_limited`, also after a reconnect, `sending` back to `waiting` on a lost connection, waiting messages kept through workspace changes while offline, `not_found` and `conflict`, the 3 retries after `rate_limited`, a late `rate_limited` for a message that shows as not sent and a second one for a message that was sent twice, and `retry`, the client's refusals, `discard`, also of the first waiting message, the drops on sign-out, another device, another account, and the end of the membership, the messages kept after a requested unsubscription, a sign-in again on the same device while offline, a renewal of the sign-in, also after an attempt refused for another GitHub account, that keeps the waiting messages and sends them again after the reconnect with the same event IDs, an identity that is not the chat's own, both switch wrappers in both directions, also with messages that are being sent or show as not sent, the overflow with only the messages whose answer may be lost sent again, also after a failed reload, for a message sent while overflowed whose answer was dropped, and across a lost connection, the reload failures (a stale one, a failure after `ready`, and the backoff reset after a success), `authorName`, a listener that throws, `close`, and that no log holds a name, an ID, or a body and the file does not log with `console`.

## Device list service

The device list service ([`src/main/devices/service.ts`](src/main/devices/service.ts); #95, decisions D4 and D5b of 2026-11-06) holds the account's devices and the end of a registration lock in force in the main process, read with the account service's `readDeviceList` (`GET /devices`), and the notices of new devices from the live `account.device_registered` frame, and it passes the device actions of the list on to the account service. [`index.ts`](src/main/index.ts) creates it after the [connection](#connection) and closes it at quit (`will-quit`). [`src/main/device-ipc.ts`](src/main/device-ipc.ts) sends its state to the renderer as `window.desktop.devices`, at most one publish every 50 ms (see [IPC contract](#ipc-contract)), and the [devices section](#devices-section) of the Account screen shows it.

- **When it loads.** Nothing is read until the Account screen asks (`load`, which the section calls when it opens). A request while no device is signed in, for example on a screen that is open during a first sign-in, an unlock, or the startup, is refused as `not_signed_in` but counts: the list then loads once a device is signed in, without another request; such a refusal sends nothing and starts no cooldown. From then on, while the application runs, the list loads again when a device becomes signed in (the condition of the workspace service: a signed-in device with a ready key and an API origin), when the connection reaches `connected`, because account frames sent while the device was offline are lost (the protocol package says that the desktop reads its devices after `auth.ok`), when `account.device_registered` or `account.device_revoked` arrives, and after a device action of the person (the account service's `onDeviceAction`: a sign-out, a removal, "This is not me", a revocation of another device, or a lift of the registration lock). The screen's request is refused within 2 seconds of the last one that loaded (`DEVICE_LIST_LOAD_COOLDOWN_MS`, `too_soon`), so that a renderer that misbehaves cannot read the API in a loop. The Project Manager kept the triggers `connected` and a newly signed-in device on 2026-11-09.
- **One load at a time.** A load asked for while one is on its way drops that answer and loads again after it, so only an answer that was asked for after the last trigger applies. While the account status shows a sign-in that runs, a load waits for its end: the account service answers reads `unavailable` for most of a sign-in (all but the browser part of a renewal, #233), and a repeated sign-in can show the device signed in before it ended.
- **Failures.** A failed load keeps what was loaded before and shows the failure as the problem. It is retried with the workspace service's backoff (`workspaceRetryDelayMs`: min(60 s, 2 s x 2^n), and 60 seconds after `rate_limited`) while connected; a lost connection cancels the retry, a load that fails offline schedules none, and the next `connected` loads. After `rate_limited`, also in the answer of a load that a later trigger overtook and that is otherwise dropped, nothing loads until the server's wait has ended, on the monotonic clock; such an overtaken answer also sets the problem `rate_limited` while nothing is loaded yet, so that the screen does not say for the whole wait that the list loads; an answer for an earlier account or device records no wait: a frame, a reconnect, a device action, or the screen's request meanwhile sends nothing, and the retry at the end of the wait loads once for all of them; a timer that fires a moment early still ends the wait. A success, and a drop, start the count again.
- **Drops.** A sign-out, another account, or another device, also a removal that waits for the API and a revocation, drops the list, the lock end, the notices, and the advice after a revocation as lost, and the answers on their way; the next signed-in device's list loads on its own once the screen asked before.
- **Notices of new devices (D4).** `account.device_registered` for a device of this account adds a notice with its ID, label, platform, and creation time; the protocol never sends it for the receiving device, and a frame that names this device adds none. The same device again replaces its notice, and the 10 newest stay (`MAX_NEW_DEVICE_NOTICES`, the most new devices that an account registers in 24 hours). A notice ends when the person dismisses it, when `account.device_revoked` names its device or a list shows it revoked, and with the drops above (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-09). A dismissal (`dismissNewDevices`) names the device IDs of the notices that the screen showed and ends only those, because publishes are coalesced: a notice that arrived after the last published state stays. A frame that arrives while no device is signed in adds no notice. The reloads that the frame itself, a reconnect, or the Account screen start do not end it, so that the person sees it also when the Account screen opens after the frame arrived. Frames are collected also before the screen asked; they then read nothing.
- **Device actions (D1).** `revokeDevice(deviceId, reason)` passes a revocation on to the account service's `revokeOtherDevice` only for an active device that the list holds now, with its ID, label, and platform, or else for the device of a notice that is held now, with the notice's label and platform: the notice came from this account's own authenticated connection, and the API checks that the device is the account's, so the notice's action works also while the list failed to load, waits after `rate_limited`, or has not loaded again after the frame. An ID that neither names, also one outside the protocol's format, and a device that the list shows revoked are `not_listed`, this installation's device (also one that the server marks `current`) is `current_device`, and without a signed-in device it is `not_signed_in`, each without asking the account service. `liftRegistrationLock()` passes the lift on, with the lock's end for the dialog, whenever the last list reported a lock, whatever this device's clock says, because the API lifts a lock in force and answers the same `204` when there was none; it answers `no_lock` only without one. The renderer keeps its clock rule for showing the line and the button. An account service that rejects makes the action `unknown`.
- **While an own action runs.** The account service holds its action lock during a revocation or a lift, also while its dialog is open, and answers every read `unavailable` meanwhile. The API sends `account.device_revoked` to the acting device too, before its answer, so a load at that moment would show "The devices cannot be loaded right now." for a moment (found in the review of #228). So while an own action runs, every trigger (a frame, a reconnect, the screen's request, a retry) waits instead, and the list loads once when the action ended: the account service's `onDeviceAction` tells the service before the action answers, and an action that ended without telling it, such as a kept confirmation, loads once for the triggers that waited.
- **Advice (ADR 0014).** A revocation as lost that the server confirmed, or whose answer is `unknown`, sets `lostDeviceAdvice` (the Project Manager's decisions of 2026-11-09). It stays through the reloads until `dismissLostDeviceAdvice()` ends it, which answers false when it did not show, or until the list is dropped; a drop while the revocation was on its way starts none.
- **State.** `{ revision, status, problem?, devices, registrationLockedUntil, newDevices, lostDeviceAdvice }`, frozen, with `onChange` listeners. `status` is `signed_out`, `loading` until the list loaded once for this device, `ready`, or `unavailable` with the read's failure as `problem`. `devices` holds the API's items in its order, the most recently created first; the list has no user ID, and the IPC view chooses what crosses.
- **Logs.** Fixed text with a failure code at most, never a label or an ID.

The tests ([`service.test.ts`](src/main/devices/service.test.ts)) use a fake account service whose reads the test answers, a fake connection client, and injected timers and a clock, and build every answer through the protocol's schema and every frame through `decodeServerMessage`. They cover nothing read before the screen asks (also with frames, a reconnect, and a device action, while the notice is kept), the state in the server's order with the lock end, frozen, the request refused without a signed-in device and within the cooldown, the reload after each account frame, a reconnect, a device action, and a new sign-in, other frames that read nothing, the dropped answer of a load that a trigger overtook, the drops after a sign-out, another account, another device, a revocation, and a pending removal, also of an answer on its way, the wait for a sign-in that runs, the backoff with 60 seconds after `rate_limited`, the retry cancelled offline and the load at the next connection, a read that rejects, the request while no device is signed in that counts without a cooldown (signed out, no device, a key that waits for its passphrase, and the startup), the wait after `rate_limited` (triggers, the screen's request, and a reconnect during it, a wait from the answer of an overtaken load and none from an earlier device, the problem shown when the overtaken answer was the very first load's, the rest of the wait, one load at its end, a timer that fires early, and a device change), the device actions (a revocation passed on only for another active device of the current list with its label and platform, or for the device of a notice while the list does not hold it, with the notice's label and platform, this device also without a list, a device that the server marks as the caller's, a revoked one, also with a notice, an unlisted one, and an ID outside the format refused without asking, the lift with the lock's end whenever the last list reported a lock, also one whose end lies before this device's clock, the refusals without a signed-in device, before the list loaded, after a sign-out, and after `close`, an account service that rejects, the triggers that wait during an own action with no `unavailable` state published and one load when it ended, also after a kept confirmation, and the advice: set after a revocation as lost and after an `unknown` one, none after any other result, kept through reloads, dismissed once, and dropped with a sign-out, another account, another device, and a drop while the revocation was on its way), the notices (the frame, none for this device and none while signed out, a replacement, the 10 newest, the dismissal of the shown notices only, with a notice that arrived since kept, the revocation frame, a list that shows a device revoked, the drops, and kept through the reloads), `close`, a listener that throws, and that no log holds a label or an ID and the file does not log with `console`.

## IPC contract

The renderer reaches the main process only through the functions of `window.desktop`. There is no `ipcRenderer` in the renderer and no function that takes a channel name.

| Channel                             | Direction                        | Arguments                       | Result or payload            | Bridge function                               |
| ----------------------------------- | -------------------------------- | ------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| `runtime:get-status`                | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the runtime status           | `window.desktop.runtime.getStatus`            |
| `runtime:subscribe-status`          | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the runtime status           | `subscribeStatus`                             |
| `runtime:status-changed`            | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the runtime status           | `subscribeStatus`                             |
| `repositories:list`                 | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the repository list          | `window.desktop.repositories.list`            |
| `repositories:subscribe`            | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the repository list          | `subscribe`                                   |
| `repositories:changed`              | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the repository list          | `subscribe`                                   |
| `repositories:mount`                | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the mount result             | `mount`                                       |
| `repositories:unmount`              | renderer to main, `invoke`       | a mount ID                      | true when it was mounted     | `unmount`                                     |
| `account:get-status`                | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the account status           | `window.desktop.account.getStatus`            |
| `account:subscribe-status`          | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the account status           | `subscribeStatus`                             |
| `account:status-changed`            | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the account status           | `subscribeStatus`                             |
| `account:start-sign-in`             | renderer to main, `invoke`       | [option]                        | the start result             | `startSignIn`                                 |
| `account:cancel-sign-in`            | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | true when one was open       | `cancelSignIn`                                |
| `account:unlock-key`                | renderer to main, `invoke`       | passphrase                      | the unlock result            | `unlockDeviceKey`                             |
| `account:forget`                    | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the forget result            | `forgetAccount`                               |
| `account:sign-out`                  | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the sign-out result          | `signOut`                                     |
| `account:remove-device`             | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the removal result           | `removeDevice`                                |
| `connection:get-status`             | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the connection status        | `window.desktop.connection.getStatus`         |
| `connection:subscribe-status`       | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the connection status        | `subscribeStatus`                             |
| `connection:status-changed`         | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the connection status        | `subscribeStatus`                             |
| `connection:reconnect`              | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the reconnect result         | `reconnect`                                   |
| `workspaces:get-state`              | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the workspace state          | `window.desktop.workspaces.getState`          |
| `workspaces:subscribe-state`        | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the workspace state          | `subscribeState`                              |
| `workspaces:state-changed`          | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the workspace state          | `subscribeState`                              |
| `workspaces:select`                 | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, [option]                    | the action result            | `select`                                      |
| `workspaces:open-project`           | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID or null, [option]            | the action result            | `openProject`                                 |
| `workspaces:create`                 | renderer to main, `invoke`       | name                            | the create result            | `create`                                      |
| `workspaces:create-project`         | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, name                        | the create result            | `createProject`                               |
| `people:get-state`                  | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the people                   | `window.desktop.people.getState`              |
| `people:subscribe-state`            | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the people                   | `subscribeState`                              |
| `people:state-changed`              | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the people                   | `subscribeState`                              |
| `people:define`                     | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, name                        | the definition result        | `window.desktop.peopleActions.define`         |
| `people:rename`                     | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, member ID, name             | the action result            | `rename`                                      |
| `people:remove`                     | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, member ID                   | the action result            | `remove`                                      |
| `people:promote`                    | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, member ID                   | the action result            | `promote`                                     |
| `people:demote`                     | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, member ID                   | the action result            | `demote`                                      |
| `people:leave`                      | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID                              | the action result            | `leave`                                       |
| `people:offer-ownership`            | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, member ID                   | the action result            | `offerOwnership`                              |
| `people:withdraw-offer`             | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID                              | the action result            | `withdrawOwnershipOffer`                      |
| `people:accept-offer`               | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID                              | the action result            | `acceptOwnershipOffer`                        |
| `invitations:get-state`             | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the invitations              | `window.desktop.invitations.getState`         |
| `invitations:subscribe-state`       | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the invitations              | `subscribeState`                              |
| `invitations:state-changed`         | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the invitations              | `subscribeState`                              |
| `invitations:load`                  | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the load result              | `load`                                        |
| `invitations:create`                | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, member ID, lifetime or null | the create result            | `window.desktop.invitationActions.create`     |
| `invitations:revoke`                | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, invitation ID               | the revocation result        | `revoke`                                      |
| `invitations:preview`               | renderer to main, `invoke`       | a pasted code                   | the preview result           | `preview`                                     |
| `invitations:accept`                | renderer to main, `invoke`       | a pasted code                   | the accept result            | `accept`                                      |
| `join-requests:get-state`           | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the join requests            | `window.desktop.joinRequests.getState`        |
| `join-requests:subscribe-state`     | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the join requests            | `subscribeState`                              |
| `join-requests:state-changed`       | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the join requests            | `subscribeState`                              |
| `join-requests:load`                | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the load result              | `load`                                        |
| `join-requests:approve`             | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, request ID, name or null    | the approval result          | `window.desktop.joinRequestActions.approve`   |
| `join-requests:deny`                | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, request ID                  | the action result            | `deny`                                        |
| `join-requests:deny-all`            | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID                              | the action result            | `denyAll`                                     |
| `join-requests:block`               | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, request ID                  | the action result            | `block`                                       |
| `join-requests:unblock`             | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, request ID                  | the action result            | `unblock`                                     |
| `join-requests:pause`               | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID                              | the action result            | `pause`                                       |
| `join-requests:resume`              | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID                              | the action result            | `resume`                                      |
| `own-join-requests:get-state`       | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the own join requests        | `window.desktop.ownJoinRequests.getState`     |
| `own-join-requests:subscribe-state` | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the own join requests        | `subscribeState`                              |
| `own-join-requests:state-changed`   | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the own join requests        | `subscribeState`                              |
| `own-join-requests:load`            | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the load result              | `load`                                        |
| `own-join-requests:create`          | renderer to main, `invoke`       | ID, name or null                | the create result            | `window.desktop.ownJoinRequestActions.create` |
| `own-join-requests:withdraw`        | renderer to main, `invoke`       | request ID                      | the withdrawal result        | `withdraw`                                    |
| `agents:get-state`                  | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the agent activity           | `window.desktop.agents.getState`              |
| `agents:subscribe-state`            | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the agent activity           | `subscribeState`                              |
| `agents:state-changed`              | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the agent activity           | `subscribeState`                              |
| `chat:get-state`                    | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the chat state               | `window.desktop.chat.getState`                |
| `chat:subscribe-state`              | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the chat state               | `subscribeState`                              |
| `chat:state-changed`                | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the chat state               | `subscribeState`                              |
| `chat:send`                         | renderer to main, `invoke`       | a text                          | the send result              | `send`                                        |
| `chat:retry`                        | renderer to main, `invoke`       | an event ID                     | true when tried again        | `retry`                                       |
| `chat:discard`                      | renderer to main, `invoke`       | an event ID                     | true when dropped            | `discard`                                     |
| `chat:load-older`                   | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the load result              | `loadOlder`                                   |
| `devices:get-state`                 | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the device list              | `window.desktop.devices.getState`             |
| `devices:subscribe-state`           | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the device list              | `subscribeState`                              |
| `devices:state-changed`             | main to subscribed frames, event |                                 | the device list              | `subscribeState`                              |
| `devices:load`                      | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the load result              | `load`                                        |
| `devices:dismiss-new-devices`       | renderer to main, `invoke`       | the shown device IDs            | true when a notice was ended | `dismissNewDevices`                           |
| `devices:revoke`                    | renderer to main, `invoke`       | a device ID, reason             | the revocation result        | `revokeDevice`                                |
| `devices:lift-lock`                 | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | the lift result              | `liftRegistrationLock`                        |
| `devices:dismiss-advice`            | renderer to main, `invoke`       | none                            | true when the advice ended   | `dismissLostDeviceAdvice`                     |

The runtime status is `{ state, revision }` (`RuntimeStatusSchema`). `subscribeStatus(listener, onError)` delivers the current status and every later one, drops a status older than the last one delivered, and returns a function that ends the subscription.

The repository list is `{ revision, repositories }` (`RepositoryListSchema`); each repository is `{ mountId, name, path, watching, state }`, and its state is `loading`, `ready` with the branch, the change summary, and the remotes, or `unavailable` with a problem. The mount result is `mounted` with the mount ID, `cancelled`, or `refused` with a reason (`MountResultSchema`); the renderer turns reasons and problems into messages ([`src/renderer/repository-view.ts`](src/renderer/repository-view.ts)). `subscribe` works like `subscribeStatus`. `unmount` passes on only a string, and the main process accepts only a UUID.

The account status is `{ revision, account, signIn, lastOutcome, deviceKey, replacementPending, apiOrigin, device }` (`AccountStatusSchema`): the account is `signed_out`, `signed_in` with `login`, `displayName`, `deviceId`, and `signInExpiresAt`, the end of the device's sign-in in UTC with milliseconds (`z.iso.datetime({ precision: 3 })`, the protocol's form, so its length is fixed; D4 on #95), or `hidden` while the person is signed out on this device or that is not known yet (#195); the sign-in is `idle`, `starting`, `waiting` with `pairingCode` and `opened`, and with `authorizeUrl`, `warning`, and `browser` (`default` when the default browser could not be opened, `other` when the person asked for the address) when `opened` is false, or `completing` after the browser's post; `lastOutcome` is null or `{ code, deviceLogin?, retryAfter? }`, where `retryAfter` is a time in UTC (`z.iso.datetime()`) that only `replacement_pending` may carry (#199); `deviceKey` is a key state; `apiOrigin` is `set`, `unset`, or `invalid`; `device` is a device state code (`DEVICE_STATES`). The start takes no argument, which opens the default browser, or the option `{ browser }` with `default` or `other` (`StartSignInOptionsSchema`), a strict object that refuses any other key and value; `startSignIn` passes on its own `{ browser: "other" }` only when the renderer asks for another browser, and nothing from the renderer (#175). The start result is `started`, `refused` with `busy`, `key_not_ready`, or `too_soon`, or `failed` with an outcome code; the unlock result is `unlocked`, `refused` with `not_required`, `busy`, `wrong_passphrase`, `passphrase_invalid`, or `too_soon`, or `failed`; the forget result is `forgotten` with `removedAtServer`, `cancelled` when the person kept the account in the native confirmation, `refused` with `busy` or `not_signed_in`, or `failed`. The sign-out result is `signed_out` or `pending`, and the removal result `removed`, `pending`, or `cancelled`; each of them can also be `refused` with `busy` or `not_signed_in`, or `failed` with a code of `DEVICE_ACTION_FAILURES`. The device list has its own channels (below); `account:list-devices` is gone. `unlockDeviceKey` passes on only a string, and the main process accepts 1 to 4,096 UTF-16 code units; the key store checks the rest. See [Account](#account).

The connection status is `{ state, reason?, retryAt?, revision }` (`ConnectionStatusSchema`): `offline` with an optional `not_signed_in` or `no_api_origin`, `connecting`, `authenticating`, `connected`, `waiting` with a reason of `CONNECTION_WAITING_REASONS` and `retryAt`, or `stopped` with a reason of `CONNECTION_STOP_REASONS`. The reconnect result is `reconnecting`, or `refused` with `not_signed_in`, `update_required`, `already_open`, or `too_soon`. Neither holds a frame, a nonce, a signature, or a key. See [Connection](#connection).

The workspace state is `{ revision, status, problem?, workspaces, selectedWorkspaceId, projects, openProjectId }` (`WorkspaceViewSchema`): `status` is `signed_out`, `loading`, `ready`, or `unavailable` with a `problem` of `WORKSPACE_PROBLEMS`; `workspaces` holds `{ id, name }` and `projects` `{ id, name }`, at most 1,000 of each (the protocol's `MAX_ANSWER_LIST_ITEMS`, the most that one answer of the API holds) with names of at most 100 characters (`LIMITS.name`), as `WORKSPACE_LIMITS` sets. The main process builds a frozen copy of the service's state for it ([`src/main/workspace-ipc.ts`](src/main/workspace-ipc.ts)): it sends no more than the limits and cuts a longer name, and a project's `createdByMemberId` stays in the main process, because no screen needs a member ID. The action result is `ok`, `waiting` with the `count` of chat messages that wait to be sent (1 to 50, `MAX_WAITING_CHAT_MESSAGES`) when nothing changed, or `refused` with a reason of `WORKSPACE_ACTION_REFUSALS`: `not_found` for a workspace that the list does not hold, a project that the selected workspace does not hold, an ID that the protocol refuses, or no signed-in device, and `too_soon` for a switch to another workspace beyond the budget. The budget, like the reconnect's, counts accepted switches: at most 20 (`WORKSPACE_SWITCH_BUDGET`) within 60 seconds (`WORKSPACE_SWITCH_WINDOW_MS`), because each switch sends an unsubscribe and a subscribe frame and each subscription makes two reads of the API; it has no cooldown, so a person can click through a few workspaces quickly, and selecting the selected workspace or a refused select is not counted. `select` takes one ID and `openProject` one ID or null, each optionally followed by the switch option `{ discardWaiting }` (`WorkspaceSwitchOptionsSchema`, a strict object with a boolean); an ID crosses as a lowercase UUIDv7 (`ProtocolIdSchema`, defined in the contract, which does not import the protocol package), and the contract refuses any other value, another UUID version, a longer string, a missing or an extra argument before the service is asked. The main process then checks the ID with the protocol's `WorkspaceIdSchema` or `ProjectIdSchema` as well. Both bridge functions pass on only a string (or null to close a project), and their own frozen `{ discardWaiting: true }` whenever the renderer's option has `discardWaiting` set to `true`; any other key of the renderer's option is ignored and never passed on. The two actions, with the ID checks and the budget, sit in one place (`createWorkspaceActions`), and every switch goes through the [chat service](#chat-service)'s wrappers (`selectWorkspace` and `switchProject`), after the ID checks and the budget: while chat messages wait to be sent, a switch to another workspace or project, or closing the project, changes nothing and answers `waiting` with their count, and the renderer asks the person and repeats the switch with `{ discardWaiting: true }` to drop them (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-05 on #27). Selecting the selected workspace or opening the open project is no switch. A switch beyond the budget never reaches the wrappers, so it drops nothing, and a `waiting` answer does not count against the budget. The renderer asks that question in a dialog of the page ([Screens](#screens)). The main process closes the open project itself, and drops the waiting chat messages without asking, when the page that may have opened it is gone: a new document commits in the main frame (`did-navigate`, also on a reload), a load of the main frame fails other than with `ERR_ABORTED` (`did-fail-load` with `isMainFrame` and an `errorCode` other than -3), the render process is gone (`render-process-gone`), or the window closes (`closed`); the workspace selection stays. Only closing the window can be asked about (the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-06 on #27): while chat messages wait, `askBeforeClosingWithWaitingMessages` holds the close and asks in a native dialog with their count, with "Close" as the default and "Keep" as the cancel. Before it asks, the window is brought to the front: restored when it is minimized, and shown when the application is hidden. "Keep" leaves the window open, and "Close" closes it, which drops the messages; a failed dialog is reported and handled like "Close", so the window can always be closed. A quit, such as Cmd+Q or Quit from the Dock on macOS, closes the window first, so the held close ends it; `followQuitRequests` notes every quit that begins, also while the dialog is open, and the answer reads that note: after "Close" the application quits once the window closed, and "Keep" forgets the quit. When the page reloads, fails to load, or its render process ends, no page is left to ask through, so the waiting messages are dropped without a question. The start of a load alone (`did-start-loading`) changes nothing, because a dropped file, a blocked link, or a blocked download starts a navigation that is then cancelled while the page stays; such a cancelled navigation fails with -3, if at all. See [Workspaces](#workspaces).

The people are `{ revision, status, problem?, workspaceId, workspaceName, ownMemberId, people, morePeople, ownershipOffer, maxUnclaimedMembers }` (`PeopleViewSchema`; #95, decision D3): `status` is `signed_out`, `no_workspace` once the list loaded and no workspace is selected, `loading`, `ready`, or `unavailable` with a problem of `WORKSPACE_PROBLEMS`, and follows the workspace state, whose revision it carries; `workspaceId` is the selected workspace, `workspaceName` its name from the workspace service's list, the same as the sidebar's, cut to 100 characters (`WORKSPACE_LIMITS.name`), and null without a selection, and `ownMemberId` the account's own person in it (the workspace service's `ownMemberId`), or null. Each person is `{ memberId, displayName, role, claimed, login, verifiedAt, former }`: the member ID crosses as a lowercase UUIDv7 (`ProtocolIdSchema`), because the people screen's actions name it, while the user ID of the account that claimed the person never crosses, and the workspace service does not keep it; `role` is `owner`, `admin`, or `member` (`PERSON_ROLES`); `login` (the protocol's login rule) and `verifiedAt` are null while the person is unclaimed and for a former member whose account's data was erased; `former` marks a removed person. The rest is as narrow as the [people actions](#people-actions) need, by the role of the account's own active person: `ownershipOffer` is the live ownership offer as `{ memberId, expiresAt }`, only for the owner, who made it, and for its target, who may accept it, and only before its `expiresAt` by the main process's clock, so null for everyone else and without an offer, and the screen hides it past `expiresAt` as well; `maxUnclaimedMembers` is the limit of active unclaimed people, from 1 to 500 (`PEOPLE_LIMITS.maxUnclaimedMembers`, the protocol's `UNCLAIMED_MEMBER_LIMIT.max`), only for the owner and the admins, who define people, and null for a member and before the workspace loaded; it comes from the last reload, and since no event reports a change of the limit, it can be out of date until the next one. The main process builds a frozen copy ([`src/main/people-ipc.ts`](src/main/people-ipc.ts)): the active people first, in the workspace service's order, then the former members, at most 1,000 (`PEOPLE_LIMITS.people`, the protocol's `MAX_ANSWER_LIST_ITEMS`), with `morePeople` counting the rest, and display names cut to 64 characters (`PEOPLE_LIMITS.displayName`, the protocol's `LIMITS.displayName`). Both channels are read-only and take no argument (the actions have channels of their own, below); there is no refresh, because the workspace service reloads the people after every `workspace.subscribed`, after a failure with its backoff, and when a live event names a person that it does not hold. A change of the workspace service, which reports every change of its people, sends the latest view once after 50 ms (`PEOPLE_PUBLISH_INTERVAL_MS`), and the waiting publish is cancelled at quit. `subscribeState` works like `subscribeStatus`. See [People screen](#people-screen). The tests ([`people-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/people-ipc.test.ts), and the people tests in [`ipc-contract.test.ts`](src/shared/ipc-contract.test.ts) and [`bridge.test.ts`](src/preload/bridge.test.ts)) use a fake workspace service and an injected timer, and once the real workspace service. They cover the sender and argument check before the service is asked, every field of a person, the workspace's name (cut, from the list, and null without a selection or when the list does not hold it), the active people before the former members, the status of every workspace state, that no user ID or device ID crosses (also with the real service, after its reload and after a claim), the offer only to the owner and its target, also a plain member, and only before its expiry, also by the injected clock, the limit of unclaimed people only to the owner and the admins, the limit with its count and the cut names, the frozen copy, one publish for a burst with the last state, `close`, a view that the contract refuses, the statuses, roles, problems, and limits against the service and the protocol, the schema's limits, and the bridge's members.

The people actions (#95, D1; [People actions](#people-actions)) have one strict channel each. `define(workspaceId, displayName)` takes the workspace ID and a display name; `rename(workspaceId, memberId, displayName)` a member ID as well; `remove`, `promote`, `demote`, and `offerOwnership` take the workspace ID and a member ID; `leave`, `withdrawOwnershipOffer`, and `acceptOwnershipOffer` the workspace ID only. Each ID is a lowercase UUIDv7 (`ProtocolIdSchema`), and a display name 1 to 64 characters (`PersonNameSchema`, `PEOPLE_LIMITS.displayName`), counted as the protocol counts them; a longer one is refused before anything else runs. The contract refuses another type, a missing or an extra argument, and an ID in capitals or of another UUID version: an empty name, a name of more than 64 characters, or an ID that is not a lowercase UUIDv7 is refused by the contract, and the call rejects, while a blank name or one with control, format, or invisible characters resolves to the refusal `invalid`. The results come from a fixed set: `{ outcome: "ok" }`, for a definition `{ outcome: "ok", memberId }` (`DefinePersonResultSchema`, `PersonActionResultSchema`); `{ outcome: "refused", reason, retryAfterMs }` with a reason of `PERSON_ACTION_REFUSALS` and a positive `retryAfterMs` for `too_soon` and `rate_limited` only, null otherwise; or `{ outcome: "unknown" }` when the answer does not show whether the API acted. Unknown keys are dropped, so no user ID crosses, and an answer outside the set is refused, not sent. The bridge passes on only strings and refuses any other value without a call.

The tests of the action contract (the people action tests in [`ipc-contract.test.ts`](src/shared/ipc-contract.test.ts) and [`bridge.test.ts`](src/preload/bridge.test.ts)) cover each channel's arguments, every refused value by position, a name at its limit in characters, every result and refusal, a retry time only for a wait, the dropped keys, each action's own channel with only its strings, and the refusal of other values in the bridge without naming them.

The join requests are `{ revision, status, problem?, workspaceId, pending, blocked, pausedAt }` (`JoinRequestsViewSchema`; #95, D3): `status` is one of `JOIN_REQUEST_STATUSES`, the statuses of the invitations, with a problem of `WORKSPACE_PROBLEMS` only while `unavailable`; each pending request is `{ joinRequestId, requestedName, login, verifiedAt, accountCreatedAt, createdAt, expiresAt }` (`PendingJoinRequestViewSchema`), with the login by the protocol's rule, and each block `{ joinRequestId, requestedName, login, verifiedAt, accountCreatedAt, createdAt, blockedAt }` (`BlockedJoinRequestViewSchema`), whose login and its times may be null; `pausedAt` is a time or null. IDs are lowercase UUIDv7s (`ProtocolIdSchema`), each list holds at most 1,000 items, and a requested name 1 to 64 characters (`JOIN_REQUEST_LIMITS`). Unknown keys are dropped, so no user ID crosses. `getState` and `subscribeState` take no argument, and `load` takes none and answers `loading` or `refused` with a reason of `JOIN_REQUEST_LOAD_REFUSALS`. See [Join requests](#join-requests).

The join request actions (#95, D1) have one strict channel each. `approve(workspaceId, joinRequestId, displayName)` takes two IDs and a display name of 1 to 64 characters (`PersonNameSchema`) or null; the bridge passes on null when the name is left out. `deny`, `block`, and `unblock` take two IDs, and `denyAll`, `pause`, and `resume` the workspace ID. The contract refuses another type, a missing or an extra argument, an ID in capitals or of another UUID version, and an empty or overlong name, and the call rejects. The results come from a fixed set: `{ outcome: "ok", memberId }` for an approval (`ApproveJoinRequestResultSchema`) and `{ outcome: "ok" }` for the others (`JoinRequestActionResultSchema`); `{ outcome: "refused", reason, retryAfterMs }` with a reason of `JOIN_REQUEST_ACTION_REFUSALS` and a positive `retryAfterMs` for `too_soon` and `rate_limited` only; or `{ outcome: "unknown" }`. Unknown keys are dropped, so no user ID crosses, and an answer outside the set is refused, not sent. The bridge passes on only strings, and for `approve` a string or null, and refuses any other value without a call.

The tests of the join request contract (the join request tests in [`ipc-contract.test.ts`](src/shared/ipc-contract.test.ts) and [`bridge.test.ts`](src/preload/bridge.test.ts)) cover every status and problem, a pause time, a block of an erased account, the dropped keys, the limits and wrong values, a pending request without its login, the arguments of each channel by position, every result and refusal, a retry time only for a wait, the bridge's members, the subscription, each action's own channel with only its values, null for a name left out, and the refusal of other values without naming them.

The creates (#95, D1 and D7; [Creates](#creates)) have one strict channel each. `create(name)` takes the new workspace's name, and `createProject(workspaceId, name)` the selected workspace's ID and the new project's name, which the renderer trims, and for a project normalizes to NFC first. Each name is 1 to 100 characters (`NewNameSchema`, `WORKSPACE_LIMITS.name`), counted as the protocol counts them; a longer one is refused before anything else runs, and the call rejects. The results come from a fixed set: for a workspace `{ outcome: "ok", workspaceId, selected }` (`CreateWorkspaceResultSchema`), for a project `{ outcome: "ok", projectId }` (`CreateProjectResultSchema`); `{ outcome: "refused", reason, retryAfterMs }` with a reason of `CREATE_WORKSPACE_REFUSALS` or `CREATE_PROJECT_REFUSALS` and a positive `retryAfterMs` for `too_soon` and `rate_limited` only; or `{ outcome: "unknown" }`. Unknown keys are dropped, so no member ID or user ID crosses. The bridge passes on only strings and refuses any other value without a call. The tests (the create tests in [`ipc-contract.test.ts`](src/shared/ipc-contract.test.ts) and [`bridge.test.ts`](src/preload/bridge.test.ts)) cover each channel's arguments, every refused value by position, a name at its limit in characters, every result and refusal of each create, a retry time only for a wait, the dropped keys, and the bridge's strings and its refusal of other values without naming them.

The own join requests are `{ revision, status, problem?, requests }` (`OwnJoinRequestsViewSchema`; #95, D3): `status` is one of `OWN_JOIN_REQUEST_STATUSES`, the statuses of the device list, with a problem of `WORKSPACE_PROBLEMS` only while `unavailable`; each request is `{ joinRequestId, workspaceId, requestedName, createdAt, expiresAt, decision, decidedAt }` (`OwnJoinRequestViewSchema`), where `decision` is one of `OWN_JOIN_REQUEST_OUTCOMES` (`approved`, `denied`, `withdrawn`, `expired`) and `decidedAt` its time, both null while the request is pending and never one without the other. IDs are lowercase UUIDv7s (`ProtocolIdSchema`), the list holds at most 1,000 items, and a requested name 1 to 64 characters (`OWN_JOIN_REQUEST_LIMITS`). Unknown keys are dropped, so no user ID or workspace name crosses. `getState` and `subscribeState` take no argument, and `load` takes none and answers `loading` or `refused` with a reason of `OWN_JOIN_REQUEST_LOAD_REFUSALS` (`not_signed_in` or `too_soon`). See [Own join requests](#own-join-requests).

The own join request actions (#95, D1) have one strict channel each. `create(workspaceId, displayName)` takes a workspace ID and a display name of 1 to 64 characters (`PersonNameSchema`), or null for the account's display name; the bridge passes on null when the name is left out. `withdraw(joinRequestId)` takes a request ID. The contract refuses another type, a missing or an extra argument, an ID in capitals or of another UUID version, and an empty or overlong name, and the call rejects. The results come from a fixed set: `{ outcome: "ok", joinRequestId, workspaceId, requestedName, createdAt, expiresAt }` for a create (`CreateJoinRequestResultSchema`) and `{ outcome: "ok" }` for a withdrawal (`WithdrawJoinRequestResultSchema`); `{ outcome: "refused", reason, retryAfterMs }` with a reason of `OWN_JOIN_REQUEST_ACTION_REFUSALS` and a positive `retryAfterMs` for `too_soon` and `rate_limited` only; or `{ outcome: "unknown" }`. Unknown keys are dropped, so no user ID crosses, and an answer outside the set is refused, not sent. The bridge passes on only strings, and for `create` a name string or null, and refuses any other value without a call.

The tests of the own join request contract (the own join request tests in [`ipc-contract.test.ts`](src/shared/ipc-contract.test.ts) and [`bridge.test.ts`](src/preload/bridge.test.ts)) cover every status, problem, and decision, a decision without its time and a time without its decision, `blocked` refused, `name_required` and `device_too_new` outside the refusals, the dropped keys, the limits counted in characters, and wrong values, the arguments of each channel, every result and refusal, a retry time only for a wait, the bridge's members, the subscription, each action's own channel with only its values, null for a name left out, and the refusal of other values without naming them.

The agent activity is `{ revision, status, problem?, workspaceId, projectId, agents, unfinishedRuns, moreUnfinishedRuns, finishedRuns }` (`AgentActivityViewSchema`): `status` is `no_project`, `loading`, `ready`, or `unavailable` with a problem of `AGENT_ACTIVITY_PROBLEMS`; each agent is `{ id, name, ownerName, disabledAt }`, and each run `{ id, agentId, state, reason, createdAt, finishedAt }` with a state of `AGENT_RUN_STATES`. The main process resolves each owner's display name with the workspace service's `memberName` (`Unknown person` for a person that the selected workspace does not hold), so no member ID crosses, and neither does a device ID, a run's project, or its repository. IDs are lowercase UUIDv7s (`ProtocolIdSchema`, decided on 2026-11-05). The main process sends at most 1,000 agents, the 200 newest unfinished runs (the model holds them the most recently created first) with the number of older ones that it leaves out as `moreUnfinishedRuns`, and the 50 most recently finished runs, and cuts an agent's name and an owner's name to 64 characters and a reason to 500 (`AGENT_LIMITS`); the view is a frozen copy. Both channels are read-only and take no argument. A change of the agent service, and a change of the workspace service, which reports a person's new name, raise the revision at once; the latest view then goes out once after 50 ms (`AGENT_PUBLISH_INTERVAL_MS`), so a burst sends one view with its last state, and the waiting publish is cancelled at quit. `subscribeState` works like `subscribeStatus`. See [Agent activity service](#agent-activity-service). The tests ([`agent-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/agent-ipc.test.ts), and the agent tests in [`ipc-contract.test.ts`](src/shared/ipc-contract.test.ts) and [`bridge.test.ts`](src/preload/bridge.test.ts)) use fake services and an injected timer. They cover the sender and argument check, the names (also of an unknown person), that no member ID, device ID, or repository ID crosses, the trim with its count and order, the cuts, the frozen copy, one publish for a burst with the last state, a publish after a new name, `close`, a view that the contract refuses, the closed lists and limits against the service and the protocol, the schema's limits, and the bridge's members.

The chat state is `{ revision, status, problem?, workspaceId, projectId, messages, moreMessages, older, olderProblem?, pending, morePending }` (`ChatViewSchema`): `status` is `no_project`, `loading`, `ready`, or `unavailable` with a problem of `CHAT_PROBLEMS`; each message is `{ id, seq, authorKind, authorName, body, createdAt }` with `member` or `agent`, the oldest first; each waiting message is `{ eventId, body, status, reason? }` with `waiting`, `sending`, or `not_sent` with a reason of `CHAT_NOT_SENT_REASONS`, in the order the person wrote them. The main process resolves each author's name with the chat service's `authorName` (`Unknown person` for a person that the workspace does not hold, `Unknown agent` for every agent until #28 names them) and cuts it to 64 characters, so no member ID or agent ID crosses, and neither does a user ID, a device ID, or a token. `pending.length + morePending` is the number of messages that a switch would discard.

- **Size.** Each view is a whole snapshot, never a delta, so that the renderer keeps the one with the highest revision, like every other state. It holds at most 1,000 messages, 50 waiting messages, and 500,000 UTF-16 code units of text in their bodies (`CHAT_LIMITS`), about 1 MB of body text, because IPC carries a string in one or two bytes per code unit. Held and waiting messages each keep at least half of this budget (the Project Manager's decisions of 2026-11-13 on #243). It is taken in three steps: the held messages from the newest back while they fit half the budget, 250,000 code units (`CHAT_HELD_TEXT_RESERVE`); then the waiting messages in the order written, which keep at least the other half; then the held messages again from where the first step stopped. A body is at most 32,000 code units (16,000 characters of up to two each), so the newest held message always shows, even when the waiting messages alone exceed the budget, and the first waiting message always shows, even when the held messages alone exceed it. While all held messages fit their half, they all show, so the waiting messages never take the older page away. The reserve is part of the budget, not added to it, and holds only the held messages that exist: what they leave of their half goes to the waiting messages, and without any held message the waiting messages may use the whole budget. So a new held message, or an older page, can push out waiting messages that were shown, which `morePending` then counts. The first message that does not fit ends each list, so the shown messages have no gap. `moreMessages` counts the older held messages that the view leaves out, and `morePending` the later waiting ones; the screen's notes show both counts. At every limit at once, a view holds 500,000 code units of body text and less than 200,000 of other strings (1,000 IDs, kinds, names of 64 astral characters, and times, and 50 IDs, statuses, and reasons), about 1.4 MB as V8 serializes it for IPC, with every `seq` and the revision as large as a safe integer can be. A body is measured with its `length`, which costs nothing. The schema checks a body's length only (16,000 characters), without the protocol's pattern, because the main process checked every body with the protocol's rules before it held it; zod counts code points only for a longer string. Snapshots with deltas would send less for each change, but need a sequence without gaps and a resync path in the bridge. With the budget and the coalescing, a view goes out at most 20 times a second, and a usual chat is far below the budget.
- **Older messages.** `older` is `none` when the view shows the whole chat, `available` when `loadOlder` can load the page before the held messages, `loading` while one is on its way, and `not_shown` when older messages exist but the view is full: it leaves held messages out, or holds 1,000. With `none` or `not_shown`, `loadOlder` answers `none` without a read, so the held history stops growing once nothing more can be shown. `olderProblem` holds the failure of the last older page; until the workspace service's backoff has passed after it (min(60 s, 2 s x 2^n) for the n-th failure in a row before this one, and 60 s after `rate_limited`), `loadOlder` answers `too_soon` without a read, so a renderer cannot read the API in a loop. The count starts again in another workspace or project, after a sign-out, and once a page loads.
- **Actions.** `send` takes one string of at most 64,000 characters (`CHAT_LIMITS.sendText`, twice the body's limit, so a body whose line breaks came as CR LF fits); the chat service prepares the body with the protocol's rules. The result is `accepted` with the event ID, under which the message shows in `pending`, or `refused` with `blank`, `too_long`, `invalid_characters`, `too_many_pending`, or `no_project` (`CHAT_SEND_REFUSALS`). `retry` and `discard` take one event ID (`ProtocolIdSchema`), which the main process checks again with the protocol's `EventIdSchema`, and answer true when the message was tried again or dropped. `loadOlder` answers `started`, `busy`, `none`, or `too_soon`. A refused call names no text or ID. The bridge passes on only a string to `send`, `retry`, and `discard`.
