- **Coalescing.** A change of the chat service, and a change of the workspace service, which reports a person's new name, raise the revision at once; the latest view then goes out once after 50 ms (`CHAT_PUBLISH_INTERVAL_MS`), and the waiting publish is cancelled at quit. `subscribeState` works like `subscribeStatus`.

The tests ([`chat-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/chat-ipc.test.ts), the switch tests in [`workspace-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/workspace-ipc.test.ts), and the chat tests in [`ipc-contract.test.ts`](src/shared/ipc-contract.test.ts) and [`bridge.test.ts`](src/preload/bridge.test.ts)) use fake services and an injected timer. They cover the sender and argument checks before the service is asked, the text limit counted in characters, the names (also of an unknown person and an agent), that no member ID, agent ID, user ID, or device ID crosses, the count limits and the text budget with half of it for the newest held messages, waiting messages that alone exceed the budget with the newest held messages, a page that fills their half, and the older page still shown, a new held message that pushes out waiting messages, the held messages after the waiting messages filling the budget exactly, a waiting message that fits exactly and a shorter one after one that does not fit, astral characters, a body that ends the list, a worst-case chat, the size of a view at every limit at once, `older` and a `loadOlder` without a read, the backoff after a failed older page and its new start in another chat, the cut names, the frozen copy, one publish for a burst with the last state, `close`, a view that the contract refuses, the statuses, limits, problems, and reasons against the service and the protocol, a body checked by its length only, the switch through the chat service's wrappers with `waiting`, the discard option through IPC, closing the project, and the budget before the question, and the bridge's members and its own fixed option.

The device list is `{ revision, status, problem?, devices, registrationLockedUntil, newDevices, lostDeviceAdvice }` (`DeviceListViewSchema`; #95, D4 and D5b, and the advice of the Project Manager's decision of 2026-11-09): `status` is `signed_out`, `loading`, `ready`, or `unavailable` with a problem of `DEVICE_LIST_PROBLEMS` (`not_signed_in`, `rate_limited`, `unavailable`, or `failed`; a read's `not_found`, which `GET /devices` never answers, crosses as `failed`). Each device is `{ id, label, platform, createdAt, lastSeenAt, lastSignInAt, signInExpiresAt, revokedAt, current }`: its ID crosses as a lowercase UUIDv7 (`ProtocolIdSchema`), because the device actions name the device by it (D1), `lastSeenAt` is null until the device first connects, `revokedAt` is null while it is active, and `current` marks this installation's device. `registrationLockedUntil` is the end of a registration lock that the last list reported in force, in UTC, or null. Each notice of a new device is `{ id, label, platform, createdAt }`. The main process builds a frozen copy of the [device list service](#device-list-service)'s state for it ([`src/main/device-ipc.ts`](src/main/device-ipc.ts)): it names each field it sends, so no user ID, key, token, or field that the protocol adds later crosses; it sends at most 1,000 devices (the protocol's `MAX_ANSWER_LIST_ITEMS`) in the service's order and the 10 newest notices, and cuts a label to 64 characters (code points, the protocol's `LIMITS.deviceLabel`; `DEVICE_LIST_LIMITS`). `getState` and `subscribeState` take no argument. `load` takes none and answers `loading`, or `refused` with `not_signed_in` or `too_soon` (`DeviceListLoadResultSchema`); the section calls it when it opens. `dismissNewDevices` takes the device IDs of the notices that the renderer showed, 1 to 10 lowercase UUIDv7s (`DismissedDevicesSchema`), which the bridge copies into a new array, and answers true when it ended a notice; it ends only those, so a notice that arrived after the last published view stays. A change of the service raises the revision at once, and the latest view goes out once after 50 ms (`DEVICE_PUBLISH_INTERVAL_MS`); the waiting publish is cancelled at quit. The bridge passes nothing from the renderer on, except the dismissed IDs and the revocation's ID and reason. See [Device list service](#device-list-service).

The device actions (#95, D1) have one strict channel each, added by the steps below. `revokeDevice(deviceId, reason)` takes one device ID, a lowercase UUIDv7 (`ProtocolIdSchema`), and the reason `removed` or `lost` (`OTHER_DEVICE_REVOCATIONS`); the contract refuses another reason, a missing or an extra argument, and an ID in capitals or of another UUID version before the service is asked, and the service checks the ID again with the protocol's `DeviceIdSchema` and accepts only an active device of its list, or the device of a notice, that is not this one. The bridge passes on only a string and one of its own two reason strings, and refuses any other value without a call. `liftRegistrationLock()` takes nothing. `dismissLostDeviceAdvice()` takes nothing and answers true when the advice showed and ended; `lostDeviceAdvice` is a boolean of the list. Both action results come from a fixed set (`RevokeOtherDeviceResultSchema`, `LiftRegistrationLockResultSchema`): `revoked` with the reason or `lifted`, `cancelled`, `unknown`, `refused` with a reason, or `failed` with a code of `DEVICE_LIST_ACTION_FAILURES`, which holds no uncertain answer; unknown keys are dropped, and an answer outside the set is refused, not sent. No result names the device, its label, or the login. See [Account](#account), "Other devices".

The tests ([`device-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/device-ipc.test.ts), the device list tests in [`ipc-contract.test.ts`](src/shared/ipc-contract.test.ts) and [`bridge.test.ts`](src/preload/bridge.test.ts), and the device list part of the test with the real session in [`account-ipc.test.ts`](src/main/account-ipc.test.ts)) cover the sender and argument checks before the service is asked (for the dismissal also an empty list, more than 10 IDs, an ID in capitals or of another UUID version, and a value that is not an array), the shown IDs passed on, the fields of each device and notice and that no user ID, key, token, or other key of the model crosses, the cut lists and labels (also with astral characters), the frozen copy, every status and problem, the limits and platforms against the protocol and the service, `load` and the dismissal, one publish for a burst with the last state to the subscribers only, a view that the contract refuses, `close`, the schemas (IDs as lowercase UUIDv7s, the limits, every time, and unknown keys dropped), the bridge's members, and that no device list that reached the renderer in a real sign-in, sign-out, and removal holds a code, a token, a signature, a key, or the user ID; and, for the device actions, the arguments of the revocation (another reason, a missing or an extra argument, and an ID in capitals, of another UUID version, or not a string), the ID and the reason passed on and nothing for the lift, the results parsed by the contract with unknown keys dropped and an answer outside the set refused, the fixed sets and the failure set against the other device actions, the bridge's own reason strings, and the dismissal of the advice: no arguments, a boolean answer, the flag in every view, and the call through the real session after a revocation as lost.

- [`src/shared/ipc-channels.ts`](src/shared/ipc-channels.ts) names the channels. It imports nothing, so the preload script uses the names without bundling zod.
- [`src/shared/sign-in-timing.ts`](src/shared/sign-in-timing.ts) holds the sign-in start cooldown, which the main process enforces and the account panel's delay for "Use another browser" follows. It imports nothing, so the renderer may import its values, like `src/shared/product.ts`.
- [`src/shared/ipc-contract.ts`](src/shared/ipc-contract.ts) holds a zod schema for the arguments and the result of every invoke channel (`INVOKE_CONTRACT`) and for the payload of every event channel (`EVENT_CONTRACT`), and the types derived from them. The preload script and the renderer import only the types, so zod is bundled into the main process alone, and the renderer needs no `jitless` setting ([ADR 0003](../../docs/architecture/decisions/0003-zod-runtime-validation.md)).
- [`src/main/ipc.ts`](src/main/ipc.ts) registers the handlers. Each handler first checks the sender: the main frame, not a subframe, still attached, at the URL the window loaded, by the rules of the navigation guard (`isAppNavigation`): in a build only `app://renderer/index.html`, during development the development server's origin ([ADR 0014](../../docs/architecture/decisions/0014-accounts-devices-and-invitations.md)). It then validates the arguments against the contract, and parses its result with the contract, so unknown keys never reach the renderer. A refused call rejects in the renderer with a message that names the channel and the reason, never the arguments. Events go only to frames that subscribed and still show the application.
- [`src/preload/bridge.ts`](src/preload/bridge.ts) builds the frozen `window.desktop` object. Its functions call fixed channels and pass no argument from the renderer on, except seven that take one string each, which the main process validates: the mount ID of `repositories.unmount`, the passphrase of `account.unlockDeviceKey`, the workspace ID of `workspaces.select`, the project ID of `workspaces.openProject`, which also takes null to close the project, the text of `chat.send`, and the event IDs of `chat.retry` and `chat.discard`; `workspaces.create` takes a name string and `workspaces.createProject` a workspace ID and a name string; `devices.dismissNewDevices` takes an array of device ID strings, of which it passes on a new array; `devices.revokeDevice` takes a device ID string and the reason `removed` or `lost`, for which it passes on one of its own two strings; each of the nine `peopleActions` takes its workspace ID, member ID, and display name strings; each of the seven `joinRequestActions` takes its workspace ID and join request ID strings, and `approve` a display name string or null; and `ownJoinRequestActions.create` takes a workspace ID string and a display name string or null, and `ownJoinRequestActions.withdraw` a join request ID string. `workspaces.select` and `workspaces.openProject` add their own fixed `{ discardWaiting: true }` when the renderer asks for it. A value of another type is refused in the preload script without a call, and extra arguments are not passed on. `account.startSignIn` passes on a fixed option of its own when the renderer asks for another browser. The functions never hand the IPC event object, which holds `ipcRenderer`, to the renderer.

To add a channel:

1. Add its name to `INVOKE_CHANNELS` or `EVENT_CHANNELS` in `ipc-channels.ts`, with a `domain:action` name.
2. Add its schemas to `INVOKE_CONTRACT` or `EVENT_CONTRACT` in `ipc-contract.ts`. Every string and array has a maximum length, and objects use `z.object`, which strips unknown keys (ADR 0003). Never send a secret: the device key, tokens, and sign-in codes stay in the main process (ADR 0014).
3. Register an invoke handler with `createInvokeHandler` in `src/main/ipc.ts` (the implementation may return a promise), or send an event through `createEventPublisher`, which checks its receivers.
4. Add a function for it to `DesktopBridge` in `src/shared/desktop-bridge.ts` and to `createDesktopBridge`. Never expose `ipcRenderer`, a channel name parameter, or a generic `invoke` or `send`.
5. Add tests: the schemas accept and refuse, the handler refuses a foreign sender and invalid arguments, and the bridge keeps its exact shape (`ipc-contract.test.ts`, `ipc.test.ts`, `bridge.test.ts`).

## Security settings

- Every window runs its renderer sandboxed and with context isolation, without Node.js integration (also in workers and subframes), with web security on, and without webviews ([`src/main/window.ts`](src/main/window.ts)). Tests lock these values.
- Developer tools are available only when the application is not packaged.
- Every web contents gets the same guards ([`src/main/guards.ts`](src/main/guards.ts)): `window.open` is denied; a navigation (`will-navigate`, `will-frame-navigate`) or redirect (`will-redirect`) of any frame to a URL outside the application is prevented ([`src/main/navigation.ts`](src/main/navigation.ts)). With the built files, only `app://renderer/index.html` itself is navigable, not other `app://` paths; with the development server, any URL on its origin; attaching a webview is prevented; and Bluetooth device selection is cancelled.
- Every session, the default session and every session created later (`session-created`), gets the session guards once ([`src/main/guards.ts`](src/main/guards.ts)): permission requests are answered with false, permission checks (for example `Notification.permission`) and device permission checks return false, HID, serial, and USB device selection is cancelled, and every download is prevented.
- The main process sets the [data folder](#local-data) and then takes the single-instance lock in it, before anything else ([ADR 0014](../../docs/architecture/decisions/0014-accounts-devices-and-invitations.md)). A second start brings the existing window to the front.
- A built application loads its renderer from `app://renderer/`, a custom scheme registered as standard and secure before the application is ready, not from a `file:` URL ([`src/main/app-protocol.ts`](src/main/app-protocol.ts)). The scheme serves only files inside `out/renderer/`. It answers methods other than GET and HEAD with 405. It answers 404 for another host, credentials, a port, invalid percent-encoding, and a path segment that after decoding is empty, ends with a dot or a space (so also `.`, `..`, and `...`), is a Windows device name (`CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1` to `COM9`, `LPT1` to `LPT9`, with or without an extension, in any case), or contains `/`, `\`, `:`, `*`, `?`, `"`, `<`, `>`, `|`, or a control character. It also answers 404 for a missing file or a directory, and for a path whose real path, after symbolic links, is outside the real path of `out/renderer/`. Every response, errors included, carries the production Content Security Policy as a header and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`; files also get their Content-Type.
- A packaged application always loads its built files. `ELECTRON_RENDERER_URL`, which electron-vite sets during development, is used only when the application is not packaged, and only for an http URL on `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, or `[::1]`.
- The renderer reaches the main process only through `window.desktop`, which holds the Electron version, the runtime functions, the repository functions, the account functions, the connection functions, and the device list functions of the [IPC contract](#ipc-contract) ([`src/shared/desktop-bridge.ts`](src/shared/desktop-bridge.ts)). The main process checks the sender and the arguments of every IPC call.
- The device key stays in the main process, in a file outside the roaming profile that the system or a passphrase protects; only on Linux, after a warning and an explicit choice, can it be unprotected ([Device key](#device-key)). No IPC channel carries it, a sign-in code, a verifier, a signature, or a token, and the store never logs. The passphrase is the only secret that crosses IPC, from the renderer to the main process ([Account](#account)).
- The renderer has no file system access. It never sends a path: folders come from the main process's dialog, and Git runs in the main process with read-only commands, no shell, and no configured program ([Repositories](#repositories)).
- The Content Security Policy is defined once, in [`src/shared/content-security-policy.ts`](src/shared/content-security-policy.ts). It allows scripts, styles, images, and fonts only from the page's own origin, which in a build is `app://renderer` and serves only the built renderer files, and no network connection: no inline code, no `eval`, and no remote origin. The main process sends it as a header with every `app://` response, and [`config/csp.ts`](config/csp.ts) writes it into the meta element of [`src/renderer/index.html`](src/renderer/index.html), whose own value, `default-src 'none'`, blocks everything if that step is missing. During `npm run dev`, the plugin adds what the development server needs: a nonce for the inline scripts and styles it injects (the React Refresh preamble and reloaded styles), new for each start, and `connect-src 'self'` for its hot reload WebSocket.

direction characters at the ends, with the same characters inside kept and refused, a huge paste trimmed without an array of the text and refused, texts that are not codes never sent, the first selection after none that keeps the code and the preview while a switch away drops them, the expiry by an injected clock (the question closed, no join and no accept, the sentence, an answer that arrives with the clock past the expiry and still offers the join, one that expires later and closes, nothing during an accept, and the next reading under a faked system time), the question before an accept, the code gone before the accept goes, every sentence, one request at a time, `unknown` sent once, "Cancel", a switch, and a sign-out with the answers after them, only the workspace's ID and name kept of an accept, and the code in no sentence, problem, preview, or console call; `useJoinInvitation`, its focus effects, its clock's timer and listeners, and the effect of `JoinQuestionBox` that calls `showModal()` and `close()` need a DOM and are not tested); `useWorkspaces`, `useHomeSignInStart`, `useChatScreen`, `usePeopleScreen`, `usePeopleActions`, `usePeopleClock`, `useInvitationsScreen`, `useInvitationsClock`, and `useInvitationActions` themselves, their effects and their dependencies, need a DOM to be tested, so the effects that pass the views and the clock to `listChanged`, the effects that move the focus to the title after a question or the code's dialog closed, the effects of `PeopleActionDialogBox`, `InvitationQuestionBox`, and `InvitationCodeBox` that call `showModal()` and `close()`, the code field's reference that "Select the code" uses, the layout effect of `InvitationCodeBox` that passes that reference and the code to `showCodeInField`, and the clocks' timers are not tested; neither is when the JavaScript engine frees the memory of a code whose last reference the renderer dropped, the agent panel ([`AgentPanel.test.tsx`](src/renderer/AgentPanel.test.tsx) and [`agent-view.test.ts`](src/renderer/agent-view.test.ts): the text of every state, another project's activity, the agents with their owners and the disabled ones, each run with its agent, its state as `data-state` and as text for all six states, its reason, the known cancellation codes (also against the protocol's `RUN_CANCEL_REASONS`, in [`config/renderer-protocol.test.ts`](config/renderer-protocol.test.ts)), and its times in a given time zone and in the system's, the main process's order, the count of older runs with its digits grouped in the locale, the subscription with a fake bridge, names and reasons as text, unique IDs, and no `style` attribute; `useAgentPanel` itself needs a DOM too), the renderer's imports (no Node.js, Electron, or network module, no file outside the renderer and the shared code, and only types from the shared code, except the product name) and its source (no network or Node.js API), the stylesheet (nothing loaded from another origin, colors only in tokens, the same dark tokens for both theme switches, the status color of each run state, a chat message body that keeps its line breaks, and a GitHub login in the monospace font), the Content Security Policy, the IPC schemas, the sender check and the handlers, the preload bridge, the runtime lifecycle with its quit handling, and the repositories: the porcelain v2 parser (renames and copies, untracked and ignored paths, conflicts, a detached HEAD, a branch without commits, ahead and behind, paths with spaces, non-ASCII characters, and line breaks, and malformed records), the remote parser and the removal of credentials, the filter driver overrides, the environment and the lookup of Git (also with Windows paths through `path.win32`), the runner with a fake `execFile` (the arguments, no shell, the environment, the timeout and output limit, the failures, and a refused folder that is not a repository), the inspector with a fake runner, the watcher with a fake `fs.watch` (the Git directory filter, also with Windows separators, a linked worktree, the fallback without recursion, close, and errors), the service with fake timers (debouncing, no parallel status, unmount, limits, duplicates, and close), the repository IPC handlers (foreign senders, invalid arguments, the dialog, and busy), and the repository panel. A test with the real `git` builds a temporary repository with a staged rename, a staged and an unstaged change, an untracked file, a remote URL with a token, and programs that would leave marker files: a filter driver, one with an empty name, one from an `include` and one from an `includeIf` file, and a file system monitor from an included file. It checks that the inspection reads the expected state, leaves every file and folder unchanged (content, size, modification time, and inode, so a lock file that was created and deleted again shows too), and runs none of the programs, while a plain `git status` runs all of them. A partial clone with an SSH remote whose SSH command leaves a marker and with the object of HEAD missing shows that neither the inspection nor its environment without `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH` (as on Git before 2.44) fetches or writes anything, while a plain `git status` runs the SSH command. The partial clone also sets `protocol.ssh.allow=always`, and the inspection's command-line options alone, without either environment variable, still allow no transport. The test also covers a subfolder, a plain folder, the Git directory, a bare repository, and a linked worktree. The device key tests use temporary folders and a fake `safeStorage` with the asynchronous API only, which encrypts with a key of its own. They cover the atomic write (a failure at each step, also after half of the data was written, keeps the old file and leaves no temporary file; an existing file at the temporary name, and on Linux and macOS a symbolic link there, makes the write fail without touching it or the target; every rename is flushed, on Windows by opening the renamed file again), that a key file holds only the encrypted PKCS#8 key (neither the key in the clear nor the public key, in any encoding), the decryption once at startup and the rewrite after `shouldReEncrypt`, the passphrase format (a round trip, a wrong passphrase, a changed file, the normalization, a lone surrogate, and the default scrypt cost), every refused key file, the system protection on each platform and each Linux backend, the Linux probe and key writes whose ciphertext starts with `v10`, that Windows and macOS refuse a passphrase or an unprotected key and a key file planted with one, the explicit choice of an unprotected key and its renewal only with a fresh choice, the move up to the system on Linux, mode 0600 on Linux and macOS (skipped on Windows), the Windows folder through an injected environment and platform, signatures that verify with `node:crypto` for every input `deviceAuthInput` builds, the replacement that keeps the previous key until it is confirmed or abandoned, also across a restart, a stop between its two steps, and a failed write, `delete`, and that no file of the store names the synchronous `safeStorage` API or `console`, and no file of the renderer, the preload script, or the shared code reaches the store. Code that calls Electron stays thin and is not tested directly; the logic it uses is in plain functions that take fakes of `ipcMain`, `ipcRenderer`, `app`, and the timers.

## Not included yet

- The content of the product screens: tasks, decisions, and granting permissions.
- The visual design (#122).
- Work for the local runtime, such as terminal and process execution and agent execution.
- Storing mounted repositories across restarts, linking a mount to a project's registered repository, and the remote URL in the cloud data model (ADR 0013 defers it to M1-05; it needs a decision on persistence).
- Writing to a repository: files, commits, and pushes, which wait for permission enforcement.
- The content of the account and join screens, and the screens of the join requests (#95): later pull requests add the join requests to the people screen, with "Deny and invite instead" composed of a definition when needed, a create, and then a denial ([Join requests](#join-requests)), and the requester's side to the join screen. On the people screen, choosing an invitation's lifetime: the screen sends none, so every invitation lasts the API's 7 days. On the account screen, beside the [devices section](#devices-section): retrying the API's refusals, and a short note that recommends two-factor authentication on GitHub (decision D6 on #95). The [sign-in renewal](#sign-in-renewal) does not take the end from `auth.ok` or reconcile it with the device list, and it does not keep the reads and writes going while its browser is open (#233). On the join screen: asking to join a workspace by its ID, and the account's own join requests with their withdrawal; the main process holds them ([Own join requests](#own-join-requests)), and a later pull request adds them to the screen.
- When creating a workspace: choosing its limit of unclaimed people, which the desktop does not send, so the API applies 500, and choosing the account's own name in it, which is always the account's display name. Renaming and archiving a workspace or a project, and opening a new project at once: it shows in the list when its event arrives.
- Stopping local execution after `revoked`: the hook is wired ([Account](#account)), and the local runtime tasks add their work to it.
- Packaging, code signing, the application name and ID, and Electron fuses (#17). With it, how a packaged application finds Git: on macOS, an application started from the Finder gets a short `PATH`, so the inspection may find `/usr/bin/git`, the stub that offers to install the Xcode command line tools when they are missing.
- Tests that launch Electron.
